## READ THIS FIRST -- PLEASE DO NOT SIMPLY RATIFY

A recent panel had seven of nine seats confirm a premise this project's author stated wrongly, and on THIS round's pass 1
the flagship seat found two false sentences in drafts that were written to CORRECT false sentences.  **Check the premises
and every sentence the diff adds, not just the derivations.**  If you cannot read files, that is expected -- the whole
diff is inlined below; name any premise you cannot settle from this packet.  Seats that read files: the diff's six files
and `GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbdrainwire_20260924/` (the pass-1 evidence) are in scope.  `regress/gate_arm.sh`

## 0. WHAT PASS 1 DECIDED (nine seats + the Opus measuring seat; Fable 5.1 last: PROCEED WITH CHANGES)

`regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py` is `#442`'s rung-3 detector for the footbridge pending-tick bound
(`src/devices/dev_footbridge.c`: `footbridge_pending()`, and the bound block in `reload_timer_value()`).  It ran in no gate
and its exemption lapses 2026-09-27.  Pass 1 wired it into gate 14 on ALL FOUR timers (one timer was measured
insufficient), rewrote its contract (the draft claimed "any RAISE above 762" -- false), repaired gate 2's R6 (the `==`
cutoff escaped every shipped #442 detector -- an L2 floor breach), and corrected three wrong records.  Filed, not fixed:
`fbboundmodes` (a bound skipped in TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC escapes every instrument), `fbdrainxdisable` (nothing proves
arm X was disabled on a correct build), `fbdrainonepass` (a single-process four-timer probe mode would halve the cost).

## 1. SETTLED PREMISES (measured unless marked read; evidence under `regress/artifacts/fbdrainwire_20260924/`)

* The bound at the probe's rate is (int)(50e6 / 65536) = (int)762.939453125 = 762.  The drained count is
  ((N-1) mod B) + 1 for N delivered ticks and bound B; N = 3051 at 4 s on an idle host (`opus5_p1.md`).
* HEAD: D = X = 3 on all four timers, VERDICT=PASS.  Bound removed (M_nobound): 3051 on all four, FAIL.
* M_en0 (`if ((nr == 0 || (d->timer_control[nr] & TIMER_ENABLE)) && ...)`): drain timer 0 PASS, timer 1 FAIL
  (X = 3051); BOTH gate-2 differentials PASS; rung 3: `CLOCK_DEAD=X` after 45 s disabled at 50 MHz.
* M_en (enable-gated, all timers): drain FAIL via arm X only; gate 2 PASS (every gate-2 arm is enabled -- read).
* M_fclk (bound only under FCLK_256): drain X = 3051 on all four; gate 2 FAILS 11 rows (its unprescaled cases).
* M_devtick (bound moved into DEVICE_TICK): drain PASS on all four (X = 1: the FREEZE sets the bound to 1 before the
  drain counts); gate 2 FAILS (R1/R2/R5/R6).
* M_x2 (`freq * 2`): drain PASS (reads 1); gate 2 FAILS (R5/R6).  M_noceil (ceiling deleted): drain PASS; gate 2 R1/R5
  fail on the two high-hz cases and **R2 prints `ok ... peak 2147483647 trough 1`** -- no overflow.
* M_nper (bound skipped in PERIODIC mode): passes the drain on all four timers AND both gate-2 differentials.
* M_eq (`==` for `>=`): passes gate 2 at HEAD (R6 peak 762) and the drain (D = X = 3).  Rung 3: a guest CONTROL write
  from 12207 Hz to 762.94 Hz left it 4529 owed against a bound of 762 (HEAD: 1).  `opus5_p1b.md`.
* R6both (this diff's R6) across 15 mutants: M_eq red (timer 2 peak 8192), M_pendguard red (timer 0 peak 4096), no
  mutant red -> green, IDENTITY 28 rows unchanged.  The one-cell alternative (owe 4096 before timer 0's re-arm) LOST
  M_pendguard.  `opus548_measured/harness_matrix.txt`.
* The `dev_footbridge.c` hunk is comment-only with the same line count.  Built with build-asan's exact flags (-g, ASAN,
  UBSAN), HEAD's and this file compile to BYTE-IDENTICAL objects; `build/` and `/tmp/gxsec-build` rebuilt md5-identical.
* `check_probe_wiring.py` as committed (HEAD + this hunk): `PROBEWIRING_PASS 71/81 detectors run by a gate, 10
  dated-exempt`; `--selftest` 22/22.  A one-line `for ...; do python3 ...` would read as UNWIRED (measured, pass 1).
* Gate 14 as committed (HEAD + this block): PASS, 1082 checks, exit 0 (`gate14_20260924/gate14.out`).  All 28 fbdrain rows green, D = X = 3 on every timer; the four drain runs took 67 s of a 4015 s gate (the rest is the pre-existing SGI/PCC2 probes).  Gate 2's fbpending section with this R6, against build/'s objects: FBBOUND_PASS, R2 ran, IDENTITY 28, R6 `timer 0 at count 0 peak 762, timer 2 owing 4096 peak 762`.

+#  The guest arms footbridge timer N at 762.94 Hz (TIMER_FCLK_256, load 256, -E cats' 50 MHz),
+#  free-runs 4 s WITHOUT acking, freezes the timer and counts the backlog one TIMER_x_CLEAR at
+#  a time.  #442's bound resets the counter to 1 once it reaches (int)762.94 = 762, so the
+#  count can never exceed 762 -- the tightest cap that cannot false-fail.

## 3. THE PRE-TABLE CHECK'S CHANNEL SECTION (written before the pass-2 kill table; `PRETABLE.out`)

```
4. CHANNEL -- per row, the mutant it kills and the observable that differs under it
   gate 14, per timer t:
     "reached a verdict"        a probe that crashes, hangs to the harness budget, or prints REPORT-ONLY (a control
                                failed / no cap parsed) -> no unique PASS|FAIL line.
     "controls prove ..."       any control failing -> FBDRAIN_CONTROL=FAILED.
     "twelve DISTINCT green ids" a control row DELETED or renamed in the probe -> 11 ids (CONTROL=OK cannot see it).
     "bounded at the asserted cap 762"  M_nobound / M_en / M_fclk / M_x8 -> BOUNDED=NO; a probe edit that parses a
                                different cap -> "(cap asserted: N)" differs.
     "both clocks alive"        a zero-tick drain (poisoned count) -> "FBDRAIN_CLOCK_DEAD=D ..." (whole-line match).
     "verdict PASS"             every bound mutant above -> VERDICT=FAIL.
     "probe exit status"        VERDICT=FAIL or a failed control -> exit 1.
   Under M_nobound the verdict, BOUNDED and exit rows vary (3051 > 762).  No row's named channel is constant under
   its named mutant.
   gate 2 R6: M_eq -> timer 2 peak 8192; M_pendguard -> timer 0 peak 4096; M_frozen -> peak 4096/8192;
     M_idx0 -> R6 (peak 1) and R6b.

```

## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""fbpending DRAIN PROBE -- a RUNG-3 detector that MEASURES the pending-interrupt
backlog of footbridge timer 1 in ~10 s, instead of waiting 43 s for it to overflow.

WHY THIS EXISTS.  The rung-3 witness (fbpending_witness.py) proves the defect by
running until the counter wraps, which takes 42.95 s at cats' emulated_hz and cannot
be made faster than 31.51 s on ANY machine (the delivery ceiling is
TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP x TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY = 68,157,440 increments per wall second, and
timer_tick0 is called from nowhere else).  A gate row cannot spend that.

THE TRICK: MEASURE THE COUNTER, DO NOT WAIT FOR IT TO OVERFLOW.

  1. The guest arms timer 1 at a KNOWN SLOW rate.  cats' emulated_hz is 50,000,000 and
     reload_timer_value() asks for emulated_hz / effective_cycles(load, ctrl, 1).  With
     TIMER_FCLK_256 and load 256 that is 50e6 / (256*256) = 762.94 Hz.
  2. It free-runs for T seconds.  Nothing acks, so the backlog is rate*T -- UNLESS the
     device bounds it.
  3. It then FREEZES the timer by writing TIMER_1_LOAD = 0x00FFFFFF while keeping
     TIMER_FCLK_256: the rate falls to 50e6/(0xFFFFFF*256) = 0.0116 Hz, one tick per
     86 s.  *** A LOAD WRITE DOES NOT CLEAR pending_timer_interrupts *** (only a
     TIMER_x_CONTROL write WITHOUT TIMER_ENABLE does -- dev_footbridge.c, the
     `pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] = 0` store in the `else` of `if (idata &
     TIMER_ENABLE)`), so the
     backlog survives the freeze.  That turns a moving quantity into a static one.
  4. It then DRAINS AND COUNTS.  DEVICE_TICK writes timer_value = random() %
     effective_cycles(load, ctrl, 0) whenever the backlog is > 0, and with load
     0x00FFFFFF that modulus is 0xFFFFFF, so the produced value is in [0, 0xFFFFFE].
     The guest writes the sentinel 0x00FFFFFF -- which the modulo CANNOT produce and
     which survives TIMER_x_VALUE's own `idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL` mask -- and spins.  A
     changed read means one tick was delivered; the guest then writes TIMER_1_CLEAR
     (one decrement) and repeats.  When the spin budget expires with the sentinel
     intact, the backlog is 0 and the number of decrements IS the backlog.

  The measured number is the whole verdict: pre-fix it is rate*T.  No wrap, no 43 s,
  and the row reports a NUMBER rather than a boolean.

  *** POST-FIX IT IS **NOT** THE CAP, AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS PARAGRAPH SAID IT
  WAS. ***  Measured by a pass-2 seat on ONE binary with ONE bound of 762: backlog 3 at
  4 s, 100 at 46 s, 375 at 0.5 s.  Once the bound is first reached the number is a
  MODULAR RESIDUE of the delivered tick count, not the bound itself.

  That is why `--expect-cap` is a one-sided `bD <= CAP` and CANNOT distinguish a bound
  of 762 from one of 7: the quantity it tests is structurally <= the bound whatever the
  bound is.  The rows that actually pin the bound's VALUE live in
  regress/diff_fbpending_bound.c (R5, an equality against the timer's own rate, and R6,
  which re-arms at a different rate) -- and they exist because a one-token mutant
  (`freq / 100.0`) destroyed 99% of the guest's clock while passing this probe.

ARM X -- the reason it is not merely a second run.
  dev_footbridge.c never calls timer_remove() and never lowers the core frequency when
  the guest DISABLES a timer: TIMER_x_CONTROL without TIMER_ENABLE zeroes the counter
  once (the same `= 0` store) and leaves the struct timer running.  So timer_tickN keeps incrementing a
  counter that DEVICE_TICK will never look at.  Arm X arms the timer, immediately
  disables it, free-runs, re-enables (which does NOT clear the counter) and then
  measures.  A non-zero result proves the counter accrues while the device is disabled.
  *** #548: THE HEADING SAID "THE PLACEMENT DISCRIMINATOR" AND X IS NOT ONE -- MEASURED.
  A bound moved into DEVICE_TICK reads X=1 and PASSES: the FREEZE's LOAD write sets the
  bound to 1 before the drain counts, so any clamp outside the callback collapses the
  backlog first.  gate 2 (diff_fbpending_bound.c R1/R2/R5/R6) catches that placement.
  What X DOES catch is a bound gated on a control bit its CTRL=0 write clears:
  TIMER_ENABLE, which gate 2 -- every arm of it enabled -- cannot see, and TIMER_FCLK_256,
  which gate 2's unprescaled cases catch too.  X is the only RUNG-3 witness of either. ***

ARMS
  D  enabled throughout.  Backlog after T seconds.
  X  disabled during the accrual.  Backlog after T seconds.  Q1's discriminator.
  T  negative control: MMU left on, so the footbridge is not mapped.  Must not return
     the device's answers.  (machine_cats.c's boot page tables map only the low 256 MB;
     the footbridge is at PA 0x42000000.)
  H1/H2 harness legs (#545) -- they REPLACED arm K, a read of IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR (+0x18c) that
     the device used to answer with fatal() then exit(1); #545 declines it.  H1: an unknown -E
     name -- no prompt anywhere in the buffer, the diagnostic naming it, exit 1.  H2: arm D's prologue verified (21 steps, r3/r5/r6/r7),
     a fresh reg, quit, then one more reg that must fail with `dead` set BEFORE close(), exit 0.
     Every surviving arm must reap exit 0.  NOT COVERED: moving s.close() ahead of that next
     send keeps H2 green (the send fails on the closed descriptor), and the legs cannot defend
     their own predicates -- harness edits, reviewed, not rowed (footbridge_sites_probe.py).

Every RAM output word is poisoned with 0xDEADBEEF first and every verdict row requires
the values it consults to be PRESENT.  Every STEPPED instruction is checked, as it
EXECUTES, against the emulator's own disassembly (the hand-assembled-encoding trap): the
21-word prologue, and arm D's drain body (0x70-0xe4) in arm E.  *** #548: THIS SAID "EVERY
PLANTED INSTRUCTION".  The accrual loop (0x54-0x6c) and arm X's disable and re-enable
words (0x54/0x58, 0x70/0x74) FREE-RUN unchecked, and on a correct build nothing proves X
was disabled at all -- it reads 3, like D.  Filed as `fbdrainxdisable`. ***

usage: fbpending_drain_probe.py <gxemul-binary> <four-byte-raw-stub>
         [--accrue S] [--expect-cap N] [--timer N] [--fast] [--only ARM] [--learn]
"""
import os, pty, re, select, signal, struct, sys, time

BIN = sys.argv[1]
STUB = sys.argv[2]
LEARN = "--learn" in sys.argv


def _opt(name, default, cast):
    if name in sys.argv:
        return cast(sys.argv[sys.argv.index(name) + 1])
    return default


ACCRUE_S = _opt("--accrue", 4.0, float)
EXPECT_CAP = _opt("--expect-cap", -1, int)   # -1 = report only
#  WHICH of the four timers.  dev_footbridge.c derives timer_nr as
#  (relative_addr >> 5) & 3, so timer N's registers sit at 0x300 + N*0x20 and
#  TIMER_1_* is INDEX 0.  A fix applied to only one of the four callbacks passes a
#  row that exercises only timer 1, which is why this is a parameter and not a
#  constant.
TIMER_NR = _opt("--timer", 0, int)
TBASE = 0x300 + 0x20 * TIMER_NR
T_LOAD, T_VALUE, T_CTRL, T_CLEAR = TBASE, TBASE + 4, TBASE + 8, TBASE + 12

CODE = 0x00008000
DATA = 0x00009000
FB = 0x42000000

EMU_HZ = 50000000.0
#  --fast drives the counter at the machine's FULL emulated_hz (load 1, no
#  prescaler = 50 MHz on cats), which is the only setting that reaches the 2^31
#  wrap inside a run: 2^31 / 50e6 = 42.95 s.  The default 762.94 Hz would need
#  32 days.  The freeze/drain machinery below is independent of the accrual rate.
FAST = "--fast" in sys.argv
LOAD_RUN = 0x001 if FAST else 0x100   # with FCLK_256 -> 50e6/65536 = 762.94 Hz
LOAD_FREEZE = 0x00FFFFFF              # with FCLK_256 -> 0.0116 Hz
CTRL_ON = 0x80 if FAST else 0x88      # TIMER_ENABLE [| TIMER_FCLK_256]
CTRL_OFF = 0x00
SENTINEL = 0x00FFFFFF                 # random() % 0xFFFFFF can never produce it
SPIN = 0x00010000                     # 65536 iterations ~ 20 device-tick periods
LIMIT = 200000
RATE = EMU_HZ / (LOAD_RUN * (1.0 if FAST else 256.0))
ONLY = _opt("--only", "", str)        # run a single arm, e.g. --only X

VENDOR = 0x1011
LOADBACK = 0x00345678
POISON = 0xDEADBEEF

# ---- RAM map (offsets from DATA) -----------------------------------------
K_LIVE, K_SRC, K_L, K_FRZ, K_CON, K_SEN, K_SPIN, K_COFF = (
    0x00, 0x04, 0x08, 0x0c, 0x10, 0x14, 0x18, 0x1c)
O_DRAIN, O_ITER, O_LASTV, O_VEND, O_PHASE, K_LIM = (
    0x20, 0x24, 0x28, 0x2c, 0x30, 0x34)

CONSTS = {K_LIVE: 0x11223344, K_SRC: 0x12345678, K_L: LOAD_RUN,
          K_FRZ: LOAD_FREEZE, K_CON: CTRL_ON, K_SEN: SENTINEL,
          K_SPIN: SPIN, K_COFF: CTRL_OFF, K_LIM: LIMIT}
OUTS = (O_DRAIN, O_ITER, O_LASTV, O_VEND, O_PHASE)

R = dict(r0=0, r1=1, r2=2, r3=3, r4=4, r5=5, r6=6, r7=7, r8=8, r9=9,
         sl=10, fp=11, ip=12, sp=13, lr=14, pc=15)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- assembler
#  Encodings are GENERATED, not typed.  Every generated word is still checked
#  against the emulator's own disassembly as it executes (arm E of the witness
#  found this class of error four times in this project).
def _imm12(v):
    for rot in range(16):
        c = ((v << (2 * rot)) | (v >> (32 - 2 * rot))) & 0xFFFFFFFF
        if c < 256:
            return (rot << 8) | c
    raise ValueError("0x%x is not an ARM immediate" % v)


def mov(rd, imm):
    return 0xE3A00000 | (R[rd] << 12) | _imm12(imm), "mov %s,#0x%x" % (rd, imm)


def movr(rd, rm):
    return 0xE1A00000 | (R[rd] << 12) | R[rm], "mov %s,%s" % (rd, rm)


def ldr(rd, rn, off):
    return 0xE5900000 | (R[rn] << 16) | (R[rd] << 12) | off, \
        "ldr %s,[%s,#%d]" % (rd, rn, off)


def st(rd, rn, off):
    return 0xE5800000 | (R[rn] << 16) | (R[rd] << 12) | off, \
        "str %s,[%s,#%d]" % (rd, rn, off)


def cmpr(rn, rm):
    return 0xE1500000 | (R[rn] << 16) | R[rm], "cmps %s,%s" % (rn, rm)


def subs1(rd):
    return 0xE2500001 | (R[rd] << 16) | (R[rd] << 12), "subs %s,%s,#1" % (rd, rd)


def add1(rd):
    return 0xE2800001 | (R[rd] << 16) | (R[rd] << 12), "add %s,%s,#1" % (rd, rd)


def br(cond, target, here):
    off = ((target - here - 8) >> 2) & 0xFFFFFF
    return (cond << 28) | 0x0A000000 | off, None


NOP = (0xE1A00000, "mov r0,r0")


def build(mmu_on=False, disable_during_accrual=False):
    """Returns [(offset, word, expected_disassembly_or_None, comment)]."""
    p = []

    def emit(off, wt, comment=""):
        p.append((off, wt[0], wt[1], comment))

    if mmu_on:
        for o in (0x00, 0x04, 0x08):
            emit(o, NOP, "arm T: MMU LEFT ON -- the negative control")
    else:
        p.append((0x00, 0xEE110F10, "mrc 15,0,r0,cr1,cr0,0", "read CP15 c1"))
        p.append((0x04, 0xE3C00001, "bic r0,r0,#1", "clear MMU enable"))
        p.append((0x08, 0xEE010F10, "mcr 15,0,r0,cr1,cr0,0", "MMU OFF -> VA == PA"))
    p.append((0x0c, 0xEE113F10, "mrc 15,0,r3,cr1,cr0,0", "C2: CP15 c1 read BACK"))
    emit(0x10, mov("r0", FB), "footbridge base")
    emit(0x14, mov("r4", DATA), "scratch RAM")
    emit(0x18, ldr("r5", "r4", K_LIVE), "C1 liveness, expect 0x11223344")
    emit(0x1c, ldr("r6", "r0", 0), "A1 VENDOR_ID, expect 0x1011")
    emit(0x20, ldr("r2", "r4", K_SRC), "0x12345678 out of RAM")
    emit(0x24, st("r2", "r0", T_LOAD), "TIMER_x_LOAD <- 0x12345678")
    emit(0x28, ldr("r7", "r0", T_LOAD), "A2 expect 0x00345678 (the handler's mask)")
    emit(0x2c, ldr("r2", "r4", K_CON), "CTRL = TIMER_ENABLE|TIMER_FCLK_256")
    emit(0x30, st("r2", "r0", T_CTRL), "TIMER_1_CONTROL <- 0x88")
    emit(0x34, ldr("r2", "r4", K_L), "load 256")
    emit(0x38, st("r2", "r0", T_LOAD), "TIMER_1_LOAD <- 256 => 762.94 Hz")
    emit(0x3c, ldr("r8", "r4", K_SEN), "sentinel 0x00FFFFFF")
    emit(0x40, ldr("ip", "r4", K_SPIN), "spin budget")
    emit(0x44, mov("sl", 0), "drained count")
/*
 *  #442 fbpending: an OFFLINE DIRECT-CALL DETECTOR for the pending-interrupt bound.
 *
 *  *** THIS IS NOT A REPRODUCTION AND MUST NEVER BE RECORDED AS ONE. ***  Under this
 *  project's witness ladder it is rung 1: it #includes the device's own translation
 *  unit and calls timer_tickN() directly, with no machine description, no address
 *  decode and no device dispatch.  It still compiles and still fails with all of that
 *  removed, which is the mechanical discriminator the ladder gives.  The reproduction
 *  is the rung-3 cold-debugger witness; this is the row that DEFENDS the fix.
 *
 *  WHY IT IS NEEDED IN ADDITION TO THE RUNG-3 ROW, measured rather than assumed.  The
 *  rung-3 drain probe measures a backlog by letting DEVICE_TICK deliver ticks the guest
 *  can see.  Two consequences, both measured against real mutants:
 *
 *    - a bound placed in DEVICE_TICK instead of in the callback (mutant m2) is applied
 *      by the very mechanism the probe uses to observe, so the probe reads a bounded
 *      counter and passes.  A direct call never runs DEVICE_TICK at all.
 *    - a bound with no ABSOLUTE ceiling (mutant m6, cap == (int)freq alone) is correct
 *      at cats' 50 MHz and overflows at a higher emulated_hz.  A guest probe cannot
 *      reach that in bounded time: the core delivers at most TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP *
 *      TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY == 68,157,440 increments per wall second, so 2^31 of them
 *      take 31.5 s AT BEST.  A direct call does 2^31 increments in seconds.
 *
 *  It compiles the SHIPPED source, not a copy of it -- a detector that restates the
 *  code it is testing is the vacuity taxonomy's first entry.
 */

#include "dev_footbridge.c"

#include <limits.h>
#include <stdarg.h>


/*  The four globals main.c owns.  Linking against every object EXCEPT main.o and
    dev_footbridge.o is what keeps this detector honest -- it runs the real timer.c
    and the real footbridge, not re-implementations -- and these are the only
    symbols that dropping main.o leaves undefined.  */
struct settings *global_settings;
int extra_argc;
char **extra_argv;
char *progname = (char *) "fbpending_bound";
size_t dyntrans_cache_size = DEFAULT_DYNTRANS_CACHE_SIZE;


static struct machine fake_machine;
static struct cpu fake_cpu;

static int failures;


/*  #442: the identity row's expected counts, named rather than written inline so a reader
    sees WHICH number moved when a row is added or removed.

    MEASURED, not predicted.  The first draft carried 18/17 from counting the rows by hand
    and both were off by one -- which is the whole argument for this row existing: a count
    a human derives is a count that drifts, and here it was wrong before it ever shipped.  */
#define IDENT_DEEP     28	/*  with R2, i.e. a depth argument was given  */
#define IDENT_SHALLOW  27	/*  without  */

static int rows;

static void row(const char *name, int good, const char *fmt, ...)
	vprintf(fmt, ap);
	va_end(ap);
	printf("\n");
	rows ++;
	if (!good)
		failures ++;
}


/*
 *  Drive one timer through `n` producer ticks and report the high-water mark.
 *
 *  The counter is read back after EVERY call rather than only at the end, because the
 *  interesting failure is transient: a bound that resets to 1 makes the final value
 *  small even when the peak was INT_MIN.  A final-value-only check would be green on
 *  the very defect this file exists for.
 */
static void drive(struct footbridge_data *d, int nr, long long n,
	int *peak, int *trough)
{
	long long i;
	*peak = INT_MIN;
	*trough = INT_MAX;
	for (i = 0; i < n; i++) {
		switch (nr) {
		case 0: timer_tick0(NULL, d); break;
		case 1: timer_tick1(NULL, d); break;
		case 2: timer_tick2(NULL, d); break;
		case 3: timer_tick3(NULL, d); break;
		}
		if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] > *peak)
			*peak = d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr];
		if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] < *trough)
			*trough = d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr];
	}
}


/*  Arm timer `nr` exactly as a guest would: a control write then a load write,
    through the device's own register handler, so the model's own interpretation of
    the two registers is what is under test rather than a re-derivation of it.  */
static void arm(struct footbridge_data *d, int nr, uint32_t load, uint32_t control)
{
	d->timer_control[nr] = control;
	d->timer_load[nr] = load & TIMER_MAX_VAL;
	reload_timer_value(&fake_cpu, d, nr);
}


int main(int argc, char *argv[])
{
	struct footbridge_data *d;
	int peak, trough, nr;
	long long deep = 0;
	double f;

	if (argc > 1)
		deep = atoll(argv[1]);

	fake_cpu.machine = &fake_machine;
	timer_init();

	printf("==============================================================\n");
	printf("fbpending OFFLINE BOUND DETECTOR (rung 1 -- a DETECTOR, not a\n");
	printf("reproduction).  TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP=%d  TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY=%.0f\n",
	    TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP, (double)TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY);
	printf("  the most one timer can be ticked in one wall second: %.0f\n",
	    (double)TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY);
	printf("==============================================================\n");

	/*
	 *  R0 -- the CONTROL that makes every row below mean something.  If the harness
	 *  cannot drive the counter at all, every bound row passes vacuously.  Drive an
	 *  ARMED timer 4096 times with the bound deliberately out of reach and require
	 *  the counter to actually move.
	 */
	d = (struct footbridge_data *) calloc(1, sizeof(*d));
	fake_machine.emulated_hz = 50000000;
	arm(d, 0, 1, TIMER_ENABLE);
	drive(d, 0, 4096, &peak, &trough);
	row("R0 the harness can drive the counter at all", peak >= 4096,
	    "peak after 4096 direct ticks = %d (expect >= 4096 with no bound)", peak);
	free(d);

	/*
	 *  R1 -- THE PROPERTY.  For each of the four timers, and for a spread of
	 *  guest-reachable (emulated_hz, load, control) triples, the counter must stay
	 *  inside [0, ceiling] across more increments than the ceiling itself.
	 *
	 *  emulated_hz is included in the sweep because it is USER-REACHABLE: main.c:376
	 *  sets machine->emulated_hz from -I, and emul_parse.c:621 from a config file.  A
	 *  cap of the form (int)freq with no absolute ceiling is correct at 50 MHz and
	 *  becomes INT_MAX at the top of that range -- which is mutant m6, and which no
	 *  row run only at cats' default would ever see.
	 */
	{
		static const struct { int hz; uint32_t load; uint32_t ctrl;
			const char *what; } cases[] = {
		    {    50000000, 0x000100, TIMER_ENABLE|TIMER_FCLK_256, "cats, load 256/256" },
		    {    50000000, 0x000001, TIMER_ENABLE,                "cats, load 1  (50 MHz)" },
		    {    63750000, 0x000001, TIMER_ENABLE,                "netwinder, load 1" },
		    {  1000000000, 0x000001, TIMER_ENABLE,                "-I 1e9, load 1" },
		    {  2147483647, 0x000001, TIMER_ENABLE,                "-I INT_MAX, load 1" },
		    {  2147483647, 0x000000, TIMER_ENABLE,                "-I INT_MAX, load 0 (2^24)" },
		    {    50000000, 0xFFFFFF, TIMER_ENABLE|TIMER_FCLK_256, "cats, slowest legal" },
		};
		const int ceiling = (int)(TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * (int)TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY);
		size_t c;
		for (c = 0; c < sizeof(cases)/sizeof(cases[0]); c++) {
			int worst_peak = INT_MIN, worst_trough = INT_MAX;
			d = (struct footbridge_data *) calloc(1, sizeof(*d));
			fake_machine.emulated_hz = cases[c].hz;
			for (nr = 0; nr < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; nr++) {
				arm(d, nr, cases[c].load, cases[c].ctrl);
				drive(d, nr, (long long)ceiling + 4096, &peak, &trough);
				if (peak > worst_peak)
					worst_peak = peak;
				if (trough < worst_trough)
					worst_trough = trough;
			}
			f = (double)cases[c].hz /
			    (double)footbridge_effective_cycles(cases[c].load & TIMER_MAX_VAL,
			    cases[c].ctrl, 1);
			row("R1 bounded across all 4 timers",
			    worst_peak <= ceiling && worst_trough >= 0,
			    "%-28s rate %12.2f Hz  peak %11d trough %11d  (ceiling %d)",
			    cases[c].what, f, worst_peak, worst_trough, ceiling);

			/*
			 *  R5 -- THE BOUND IS THE TIMER'S OWN RATE, AS AN EQUALITY.
			 *
			 *  *** R1 ABOVE IS AN INEQUALITY AGAINST A CONSTANT, AND A
			 *  PASS-2 SEAT MEASURED FIVE MUTANTS WALKING THROUGH IT. ***  It
			 *  computes `f` -- the case's own delivered rate -- PRINTS it,
			 *  and then compares nothing to it, so ANY bound between R0's
			 *  floor and the ceiling satisfies it.
			 *
			 *  The one that matters is `double bound = freq / 100.0;` -- ONE
			 *  TOKEN.  Measured at rung 3 with a 0.5 s accrual: the shipped
			 *  build owes the guest 375 interrupts and that mutant delivers
			 *  FOUR.  It destroys 99% of the guest's clock and BOTH detectors
			 *  said PASS.  That is exactly the failure timer.h's "THE BACKLOG
			 *  IS RETAINED" paragraph exists to prevent.
			 *
			 *  An equality closes it, needs no new oracle, and costs nothing:
			 *  `f` was already computed for the message above.
			 */
			{
				int want = (int) f;
				if (want < 1)
					want = 1;
				if (want > ceiling)
					want = ceiling;
				row("R5 the bound IS this timer's rate",
				    worst_peak == want,
				    "%-28s peak %11d  want %11d", cases[c].what,
				    worst_peak, want);
			}
			/*
			 *  R3 -- ONE INTERRUPT IS STILL OWED, and it is a separate row
			 *  from R1 because a bound that resets to ZERO satisfies R1
			 *  perfectly while dropping an interrupt the guest was owed.
			 *  luna88k resets to 1 for exactly this reason
			 *  (dev_luna88k.c:255-257: "restart the nr of pending
			 *  interrupts", not "forget them").  Measured: this row is the
			 *  only one of the four that separates reset-to-1 from
			 *  reset-to-0, and the rung-3 guest probe cannot see the
			 *  difference at all.
			 */
			row("R3 the reset still owes one interrupt",
			    worst_trough >= 1,
			    "%-28s lowest value the counter ever held: %d",
			    cases[c].what, worst_trough);
			free(d);
		}
	}

	/*
	 *  R2 -- NO SIGNED OVERFLOW, stated as its own row because R1's ceiling is a
	 *  CHOICE and this one is not.  2^31 + 4096 increments at the worst-case rate;
	 *  the counter must never be negative.  Skipped unless a depth is given, because
	 *  it costs a few seconds -- but the default depth is still above any cap the
	 *  fix could reasonably choose.
	 */
	if (deep > 0) {
		d = (struct footbridge_data *) calloc(1, sizeof(*d));
		fake_machine.emulated_hz = 2147483647;
		arm(d, 0, 1, TIMER_ENABLE);
		drive(d, 0, deep, &peak, &trough);
		row("R2 no signed overflow", trough >= 0 && peak >= 0,
		    "%lld direct ticks: peak %d trough %d", deep, peak, trough);
		free(d);
	} else {
		printf("  --   R2 no signed overflow                       "
		       "SKIPPED (pass a depth, e.g. 2147487744)\n");
	}

	/*
	 *  R6 -- THE BOUND IS RECOMPUTED ON RE-ARM, AND IT IS PER TIMER.
	 *
	 *  *** THE CASE LOOP ABOVE ARMS ALL FOUR TIMERS WITH IDENTICAL PARAMETERS AND
	 *  ARMS EACH EXACTLY ONCE, so it is structurally blind to two mutants a pass-2
	 *  seat measured walking through everything: *** a bound FROZEN after the first
	 *  arm (recomputation stops mattering) and a bound written to `[0]` instead of
	 *  `[timer_nr]` (every timer shares timer 0's).  Neither can be seen by a table
	 *  where all four timers always agree and nothing is ever re-armed.
	 *
	 *  So: arm two timers at DIFFERENT rates, then RE-ARM one at a third, and
	 *  require each timer's bound to track its own current rate.  Same oracle as
	 *  R5 -- the peak the counter reaches -- so no new oracle is introduced.
	 *
	 *  *** #548: A RE-ARM HAS TWO CELLS AND THIS ROW BUILT ONE. ***  The callback's
	 *  `>=` splits a re-arm by where the count sits against the NEW bound: below it
	 *  (timer 0, re-armed at count 0) or already past it (timer 2, re-armed with
	 *  4096 owed).  `==` for `>=` is identical in the first cell and never fires
	 *  again in the second; it passed every row in the battery, and one guest
	 *  CONTROL write left it 4529 owed against a bound of 762 (measured).  Owing
	 *  4096 before timer 0's own re-arm would have TRADED the cells, not added one:
	 *  a bound recomputed only while a backlog is owed then passed.  Both, one row.
	 */
	{
		int peak, trough, peak2, trough2;
		struct footbridge_data *r6 =
		    (struct footbridge_data *) calloc(1, sizeof(*r6));

		fake_machine.emulated_hz = 50000000;
		arm(r6, 0, 1, TIMER_ENABLE);			/*  50 MHz  */
		arm(r6, 1, 256, TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256);	/*  762 Hz  */
		arm(r6, 2, 1, TIMER_ENABLE);			/*  50 MHz  */
		drive(r6, 2, 4096, &peak2, &trough2);		/*  4096 owed  */

		/*  RE-ARM timer 0 much slower.  A frozen bound keeps 50e6 here.  */
		arm(r6, 0, 256, TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256);
		drive(r6, 0, 4096, &peak, &trough);
		/*  and timer 2, whose debt is ABOVE the new bound when it lands.  */
		arm(r6, 2, 256, TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256);
		drive(r6, 2, 4096, &peak2, &trough2);
		row("R6 a re-armed timer's bound follows its NEW rate",
		    peak == 762 && peak2 == 762,
		    "re-armed 50 MHz -> 762 Hz: timer 0 at count 0 peak %d, timer 2 owing "
		    "4096 peak %d; want 762", peak, peak2);

		/*  And timer 1 must carry ITS own bound, not timer 0's.  */
		drive(r6, 1, 4096, &peak, &trough);
		row("R6b each timer carries its OWN bound",
		    peak == 762, "timer 1: peak %d want 762", peak);
		free(r6);
	}

	/*
	 *  @@SELFCHECK@@ -- the SECOND failability control, asking a different question
	 *  from the self-mutant in gate_offline.sh.  That one breaks dev_footbridge.c and
	 *  demands a NAMED row notice, so it catches a fixture that has stopped reaching
	 *  the code.  This one breaks nothing and feeds row() itself a deliberate
	 *  mismatch, so it catches a comparator edited into always-passing -- a state in
	 *  which every row prints `ok` and the row count AND the verdict token both
	 *  survive.  Neither catches the other's failure.
	 *
	 *  The token in the row name is load-bearing: selfmutant.py filters exactly it,
	 *  and without it these deliberate FAIL lines would be read as a mutant kill for
	 *  a mutant nothing caught.
	 */
	{
		int sc_f = failures, sc_r = rows, sc_bad = 0;

		row("@@SELFCHECK@@/false", 0, "a deliberate mismatch");
		if (failures <= sc_f)
			sc_bad++;
		failures = sc_f; rows = sc_r;

		row("@@SELFCHECK@@/true", 1, "a deliberate match");
		if (failures != sc_f)
			sc_bad++;
		failures = sc_f; rows = sc_r;

		/*  sc_bad is a SEPARATE local that survives the restore above -- restoring
		    `failures` is what makes the sentinel non-destructive, so the evidence
		    cannot live in `failures` itself.  */
		row("SELFCHECK the comparator can still fail", sc_bad == 0,
		    "both directions");
	}

	/*
	 *  R4 -- THE AGGREGATE: red whenever any case above is.  #442 added it as the
	 *  self-mutant lane's pin, believing R1/R3 -- printed once per emulated_hz/load
	 *  case -- could not be pinned.  They can: selfmutant.py matches the pin anywhere
	 *  in the printed line, so one case's R3 row is unique.  And an aggregate pin is
	 *  VERDICT-EQUIVALENT, so #537 pins R3's "cats, slowest legal" row instead;
	 *  R4 stays as the every-case summary.
	 */
	row("R4 every case bounded", failures == 0,
	    "%d row failure(s) above", failures);

	/*
	 *  IDENTITY -- the row count asserts itself.  Without it, DELETING a row is
	 *  invisible: every remaining row prints ok, the verdict token survives, and the
	 *  gate's `rows actually run` floor only notices a drop BELOW the floor.  This is
	 *  the constblind shape, and gate section E hard-fails a new differential lacking
	 *  it.
	 *
	 *  `rows + 1` counts this row itself, which has not been printed yet -- arguments
	 *  are evaluated before the call.  The expectation is CONDITIONAL on `deep`,
	 *  because R2 only runs when a depth is given, and hardcoding one value would make
	 *  a no-depth run red for no defect.  The SELFCHECK block restores `rows`, so its
	 *  two deliberate rows do not count while its verdict row does.
	 */
	row("IDENTITY: row count", rows + 1 == (deep > 0 ? IDENT_DEEP : IDENT_SHALLOW),
	    "%d rows (expect %d with R2, %d without)",
	    rows + 1, IDENT_DEEP, IDENT_SHALLOW);

	/*  #537: the house verdict token, `<WORD>_PASS' / `<WORD>_FAIL'.  This printed
	    `FBBOUND_VERDICT=PASS', which selfmutant.py's `\b([A-Z0-9_]+_PASS)\b' can never
	    match -- the one deviation that kept this harness's self-mutant lane out of gate 2
	    since #442 (diagnosed as `fbsmlane', 2026-08-27).  gate_offline.sh greps this line.  */
	printf("\nFBBOUND_%s\n", failures ? "FAIL" : "PASS");
	return failures ? 1 : 0;
}

# CHECKPOINT -- 2026-09-24 ~03:20 (087a90 sections rewritten; the peer's sections below are untouched)

VERDICT: PROCEED WITH CHANGES

The wiring is worth doing. The drain probe's arm X is the only thing in the battery that kills a bound that applies only while the timer is enabled; gate 2 is blind to it (measured). But as written the proposal checks one timer, uses three aggregate rows, and carries a contract with two false sentences.

**1. Test-first half: MEASURED, and the prediction held.**
- **Setup:** HEAD bead6ad9, built in a scratch clone. I ran exactly `python3 -u fbpending_drain_probe.py <bin> <stub> --expect-cap 762 [--timer N]`, using the stub written the way gate_arm.sh's STUB block writes it (bytes `00 00 a0 e1`).
- **HEAD, default and `--timer 0,1,2,3` (5 runs):** `FBDRAIN_CONTROL=OK`, `BACKLOG_D=3`, `BACKLOG_X=3`, `CLOCK_DEAD=` (empty), `BOUNDED=YES (cap asserted: 762)`, `VERDICT=PASS`, exit 0.
- **Mutant with the bound removed, all four timers:** `D=3051`, `X=3051`, `VERDICT=FAIL`, exit 1.
- **Why the count is 3051:** it is exactly floor(4.000 s A- 762.94). The delivered tick count follows the SIGALRM clock (260 signals), not the harness's 4.02 s. Every idle run gave 3051.
- **PROBE_WALL is never printed when `--expect-cap` is set.** The probe calls `sys.exit` at the verdict, before the print: 0 of 4 capped logs have the line. The report-only run printed `PROBE_WALL=16.7s`.
- **Cost:** 16.7 s per run measured externally, so four timers take 66.8 s.

**2. Q1, premise audit.**
- **The arithmetic is right.** (int)762.939453125 = 762. The ceiling (68,157,440) does not engage. After the fix the counter ranges over [1, 762], so 762 is the tightest cap that cannot false-fail. `-E cats` gets 50 MHz only because no `-I` is given.
- **The probe does arm other rates.** All three of these happen in the single-stepped prologue, while timer callbacks are stopped:
  - LOAD�+?0x12345678 under DEVINIT's control 0x40: 14.58 Hz, bound 14 (this is also where the struct timer is created).
  - CONTROL�+?0x88: 0.057 Hz, bound 1.
  - LOAD�+?256: 762.94 Hz, bound 762.
  - No tick lands during those transients: N is exactly 3051.
- **There is a fourth rate:** the FREEZE sets the bound to 1 for the entire drain. That is what causes finding 5.
- **Loaded host (probe and emulator pinned to one core with competing busy loops):**

  | busy loops on the core | bound-removed mutant (D/X) | HEAD (D/X) |
  |---|---|---|
  | 3 | 3049/3052, FAIL | 5/30, PASS |
  | 12 (about 1/13 of a core) | 1183/1183, FAIL (1.55A- margin) | 385/379, PASS |

- **So "always exceeds 762" is not guaranteed.** When the emulator is starved it accrues fewer ticks. The failure mode is losing teeth silently, never a false red. Say so in the contract.

**3. Q2: one timer is not enough. MEASURED.**
- **The brief's premise is wrong.** A per-timer defect does not need a callback-level edit. `footbridge_pending` takes `nr`, so one conjunct inside it is enough.
- **`if (nr == 0 && �?�)`:** passes timer 0 (3/3) and fails timers 1�?"3 (3051). Gate 2 catches it anyway.
- **The case that matters:** `if ((nr == 0 || (d->timer_control[nr] & TIMER_ENABLE)) && �?�)`.
  - Passes on timer 0 (3/3) and fails on timer 1 (X=3051).
  - Both gate 2 differentials pass: diff_fbpending_bound gives `FBBOUND_PASS`, diff_footbridge gives 864/864.
  - It breaks the clock again, as #442 did, at rung 3: 45 s disabled at 50 MHz on timer 1 gives `CLOCK_DEAD=X`. HEAD under the same command reads 3485 and is alive.
- **Consequence:** with a timer-0-only row, nothing in the battery sees this. Run all four timers, for about 50 s more.

**4. Q3: yes, single edits get past the proposed rows and bring #442 back. MEASURED.**

| edit | drain probe (proposed rows) | gate 2 | brings the dead clock back? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `!(d->timer_control[nr] & TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC) &&` (bound skipped in periodic mode) | **PASS on all 4 timers** (3/3) | **PASS in both differentials** | yes, rung 3 |
| Bound moved from the callback into DEVICE_TICK's enabled branch (two hunks) | **PASS on all 4 timers** (1/1) | FAIL (R1/R2/R5/R6) | yes, rung 3 |
| `(d->timer_control[nr] & TIMER_ENABLE) &&` | FAIL, and only through arm X (3/3051) | **PASS (blind)** | yes, rung 3 |
| `bound = freq * 2.0` | PASS (1/1) | FAIL (R5/R6) | no |
| Ceiling deleted | PASS | FAIL (R1/R5) | no (see finding 8) |

- **The periodic-mode edit gets past every row in the battery.** Neither instrument ever sets PERIODIC, FCLK_16 or EXTERNAL: the probe uses 0x88 and 0x00, gate 2 uses 0x80 and 0x88. Yet DEVINIT resets every timer to periodic mode.
  - A scratch copy of the probe with one constant changed to add 0x40: HEAD passes (3/3), the mutant fails (D=3051).
  - With that variant, `--fast --accrue 45 --only D` gives `CLOCK_DEAD=D`; HEAD reads 3455 and is alive.
  - This is coverage debt, outside the carrier's DEPENDS-ON enumeration. Disclose it and file it. The cheapest fix is one periodic case in `diff_fbpending_bound.c`'s R1 table.
- **I predicted the FCLK_256-only conjunct would also get through. It does not:** arm X's CTRL=0 write clears that bit too (X=3051 on all four timers), and gate 2 catches it as well.

**5. The contract and the probe header make false claims. MEASURED.**
- **"Catches any RAISE above 762" is false.** What the rows see is ((N�^'1) mod B)+1, so a raised bound below the tick count wraps around: doubling the bound (B=1525) reads 1/1 and passes.
- **The contract leaves out** the DEVICE_TICK placement and the control-register axis (finding 4).
- **Arm X's "PLACEMENT DISCRIMINATOR" text is false.** The DEVICE_TICK mutant reads X=1 and passes. The freeze's LOAD write sets the bound to 1, so any clamp outside the callback collapses the backlog before it is counted. What X actually catches is a bound that depends on control bits X's CTRL=0 write clears (the enable-only and FCLK_256-only bounds).

**6. Q4: row design.**
- **Every mutant this probe caught shows up in the VERDICT row alone.** In every run, `VERDICT=PASS` held exactly when the exit code was 0, CONTROL was OK, CLOCK_DEAD was empty and both backlogs were �% 762. Rows 1 and 3 add attribution only.
- **Row 3 is dangerous if written as an empty-string compare.** `v=$(sed -n 's/^FBDRAIN_CLOCK_DEAD=//p' log); [ "$v" = "" ]` is green on a log that has no such line at all (I demonstrated this). Use `grep -cx 'FBDRAIN_CLOCK_DEAD=' = 1`.
- **The brief quotes only the first 3 lines of a roughly 90-line block.** Idioms it did not name:
  - a completion guard. The probe has no `*_DONE` line; with the cap set, `grep -c '^FBDRAIN_VERDICT=' = 1` can serve.
  - checks per named control row plus a pinned denominator. There are 12 rows: R0 C1 C2 A1 A2 E H1 H2 T L1 L2 P. `CONTROL=OK` is an aggregate and stays OK if a row is deleted from the probe.
  - xscale's `|| xrc=$?` exit-status row.
  - `grep -cx 'FBDRAIN_BOUNDED=YES  (cap asserted: 762)'`, to pin the cap the probe actually parsed.
- **Other details:**
  - Pass `--accrue 4` explicitly.
  - Do not echo the MEASURED block: after the fix it prints "measured accrual rate 0.7 Hz".
  - `--only` cannot carry a row. `--only X --timer 1 --expect-cap 762` gives `CONTROL=FAILED`, `VERDICT=REPORT-ONLY`, exit 1.
- **Gate 14 is the right home.** It already runs `-E cats` with the same stub. But `gatedevicesplit` has been OWED since #498, and the #497/#498 text in gate_arm.sh says not to compound it silently, so the new block must say so.
- **Other units' edits are uncommitted in the same files.** The working copies carry another unit's uncommitted `crime_width` section in gate_arm.sh and 156 uncommitted lines in check_probe_wiring.py. Stage hunks selectively.

**7. Q5: records. MEASURED with `check_probe_wiring.py` on the clone.**
- Wired, entry kept: `STALE EXEMPTION �?� RUN by gate_arm.sh:1776`. This confirms the brief's premise.
- Wired, entry deleted: `PASS 71/81, 10 dated-exempt`.
- **A one-line `for t in 0 1 2 3; do python3 �?�; done` reads as `UNWIRED DETECTOR`.** The command-position regex rejects the `for �?�;` prefix, so `python3` must start its own line.
- A timer-0-only call passes: section R cannot tell one timer from four.
- **On 2026-09-28 it still goes red, with 10 expired entries** (11 at HEAD). #548 clears 1 of the 11 exemptions that lapse that day.
- `--selftest` on the edited checker: 22/22.
- `GATE_MANIFEST` stays 16, and nothing else pins gate 14's check count.
- Text still owed: the probe header's arm X paragraph, `fable_queue.md:516`, and a pointer from `gate_offline.sh:1311`.

**8. Something else: two false statements about the ceiling. MEASURED with gate 2's own harness.**
- **The claims:** `dev_footbridge.c` (in reload_timer_value) says deleting the ceiling means "the overflow returns". The #442 block in `gate_offline.sh` says the no-ceiling mutant fails R2.
- **What I measured:** with the ceiling deleted, run at depth 2147487744, R2 prints `ok R2 no signed overflow �?� peak 2147483647 trough 1`.
- **Why:** the comparison runs before the increment and resets the counter to 1, so it fires at INT_MAX and no int-sized bound can overflow. The ceiling limits how large the backlog can get (R1/R5 go red at high emulated_hz); it does not prevent a wrap. Both are wrong records (L4); file them, and do not cite the ceiling deletion as a #442 regression.

**9. Traps in the build recipe the seats were given (all bit me).**
- On this machine `git clone` checks files out with CRLF; `-c core.autocrlf=false` is needed.
- `--shared` from a `C:/` path records a Windows path for git's shared object store, so every git command inside WSL fails. My scripted restore refused and kept the sentinel. I restored from git-bash and cleared the sentinel only after the two-part check: source `cmp` against the HEAD blob, and the rebuilt binary's md5 equal to the base.
- Redirecting `wsl.exe` output on the git-bash side lost stdout lines. I re-derived the mutant build status from artifacts: 8 distinct md5s, all different from the base.
- `regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py` is committed with CRLF line endings. That is harmless unless someone executes it directly.

**Commands run.** Reads used `git show HEAD:` or `git grep` against GXEMUL-SEC; I wrote nothing there. All work ran as `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1 wsl -d Gentoo -- bash /mnt/c/.../_scratchpad/opus548/scripts/<script>`:

VERDICT: The `==` mutant passes every gated row at HEAD and was not detected until the R6 repair. **1. M_eq at HEAD. MEASURED.** Your reading is confirmed.
- **Gate 2, bound harness:** `diff_fbpending_bound 2147487744` gives `FBBOUND_PASS`, with R6 peak 762 and 0 failing rows.
- **Gate 2, footbridge harness:** `diff_footbridge.c` gives 864 rows, 0 failures, `DIFF_FOOTBRIDGE_PASS`. It calls `timer_tickN` and `footbridge_pending` zero times, so it cannot see this at all.
- **Drain probe** (`--expect-cap 762 --accrue 4 --timer 0`): `CONTROL=OK`, D=3, X=3, `VERDICT=PASS`, exit 0. That is identical to the HEAD binary under the same instrument.
- **Why your reading holds:** every arm in the harness is a fresh `calloc` with count 0 (R0, R1, R2, and R6's re-arm). The probe's prologue re-arms three times at count 0 while stepping. The FREEZE lowers the bound to 1 over a residue of 3, but at 0.0116 Hz no callback lands in a 0.5 s drain slice, because each `continue` resets `next_tick_at` to one 86 s interval.

| M_en, M_en0, M_nper | PASS | PASS | PASS (not R6's job) |

- **IDENTITY** is ok in all 60 runs; the row count is unchanged at 28.
- **None of the first-pass mutants moves from red to green** under any version.
- **M_frozen and M_idx0** remain red under R6 and R6b respectively, as you required.

**3. Your "at least as strong" argument does not hold. MEASURED.**
- **The mutant:** M_pendguard recomputes the bound on re-arm only when a backlog exists: `if (d->timer[timer_nr] == NULL || d->pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] != 0) d->pending_bound[timer_nr] = (int) bound;`
- **What happens:** R6old detects it (peak 4096). Under R6new the whole harness passes, and `diff_footbridge` passes too.
- **The mirror case:** M_idle (store only when the count is 0) passes R6old and is caught by R6new.
- **The reason:** the body's `>=` splits a re-arm into two cells, count below the new bound and count above it. R6old covers only the first and R6new only the second. R6both covers both and catches the union of what the other two catch.
- **Recommendation:** adopt R6both, not the one-liner. It adds no row and no oracle. Two cautions:
  - `diff_fbpending_bound.c` is committed with CRLF line endings, so the edit must keep them.
  - Nothing pins R6's text; I grepped.

**4. Rung-3 consequence of M_eq. MEASURED, and cheap.** I wrote a scratch driver that reuses the frozen probe's own machinery and adds a five-word re-arm stub; its encodings were checked as they executed, with 0 mismatches.
- **The sequence:** arm at 12207 Hz, accrue 0.25 s without acking, then a guest CONTROL write to ENABLE|FCLK_256 (762.94 Hz, bound 762). That write does not clear the count. Accrue 2 s more, then freeze and drain.
- **HEAD:** backlog 1. The count reset at the first slow callback.
- **M_eq:** backlog 4529, which is exactly 3004 + 1525 ticks and above the new bound of 762. All controls were OK in both runs.
- **Full wrap attempt, inconclusive:** 50 MHz for 1 s, then a LOAD write to 2 (25 MHz), then 100 s. Neither binary showed a dead clock: M_eq hit the drain limit of 200000, and HEAD drained 3390. I did not pursue it further.

bead6ad9
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""fbpending DRAIN PROBE -- a RUNG-3 detector that MEASURES the pending-interrupt
backlog of footbridge timer 1 in ~10 s, instead of waiting 43 s for it to overflow.

WHY THIS EXISTS.  The rung-3 witness (fbpending_witness.py) proves the defect by
running until the counter wraps, which takes 42.95 s at cats' emulated_hz and cannot
be made faster than 31.51 s on ANY machine (the delivery ceiling is
TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP x TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY = 68,157,440 increments per wall second, and
timer_tick0 is called from nowhere else).  A gate row cannot spend that.

THE TRICK: MEASURE THE COUNTER, DO NOT WAIT FOR IT TO OVERFLOW.

  1. The guest arms timer 1 at a KNOWN SLOW rate.  cats' emulated_hz is 50,000,000 and
     reload_timer_value() asks for emulated_hz / effective_cycles(load, ctrl, 1).  With
     TIMER_FCLK_256 and load 256 that is 50e6 / (256*256) = 762.94 Hz.
  2. It free-runs for T seconds.  Nothing acks, so the backlog is rate*T -- UNLESS the
     device bounds it.
  3. It then FREEZES the timer by writing TIMER_1_LOAD = 0x00FFFFFF while keeping
     TIMER_FCLK_256: the rate falls to 50e6/(0xFFFFFF*256) = 0.0116 Hz, one tick per
     86 s.  *** A LOAD WRITE DOES NOT CLEAR pending_timer_interrupts *** (only a
     TIMER_x_CONTROL write WITHOUT TIMER_ENABLE does -- dev_footbridge.c, the
     `pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] = 0` store in the `else` of `if (idata &
     TIMER_ENABLE)`), so the
     backlog survives the freeze.  That turns a moving quantity into a static one.
  4. It then DRAINS AND COUNTS.  DEVICE_TICK writes timer_value = random() %
     effective_cycles(load, ctrl, 0) whenever the backlog is > 0, and with load
     0x00FFFFFF that modulus is 0xFFFFFF, so the produced value is in [0, 0xFFFFFE].
     The guest writes the sentinel 0x00FFFFFF -- which the modulo CANNOT produce and
     which survives TIMER_x_VALUE's own `idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL` mask -- and spins.  A
     changed read means one tick was delivered; the guest then writes TIMER_1_CLEAR
     (one decrement) and repeats.  When the spin budget expires with the sentinel
     intact, the backlog is 0 and the number of decrements IS the backlog.

  The measured number is the whole verdict: pre-fix it is rate*T.  No wrap, no 43 s,
  and the row reports a NUMBER rather than a boolean.

  *** POST-FIX IT IS **NOT** THE CAP, AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS PARAGRAPH SAID IT
  WAS. ***  Measured by a pass-2 seat on ONE binary with ONE bound of 762: backlog 3 at
  4 s, 100 at 46 s, 375 at 0.5 s.  Once the bound is first reached the number is a
  MODULAR RESIDUE of the delivered tick count, not the bound itself.

  That is why `--expect-cap` is a one-sided `bD <= CAP` and CANNOT distinguish a bound
  of 762 from one of 7: the quantity it tests is structurally <= the bound whatever the
  bound is.  The rows that actually pin the bound's VALUE live in
  regress/diff_fbpending_bound.c (R5, an equality against the timer's own rate, and R6,
  which re-arms at a different rate) -- and they exist because a one-token mutant
  (`freq / 100.0`) destroyed 99% of the guest's clock while passing this probe.

ARM X -- the reason it is not merely a second run.
  dev_footbridge.c never calls timer_remove() and never lowers the core frequency when
  the guest DISABLES a timer: TIMER_x_CONTROL without TIMER_ENABLE zeroes the counter
  once (the same `= 0` store) and leaves the struct timer running.  So timer_tickN keeps incrementing a
  counter that DEVICE_TICK will never look at.  Arm X arms the timer, immediately
  disables it, free-runs, re-enables (which does NOT clear the counter) and then
  measures.  A non-zero result proves the counter accrues while the device is disabled.
  *** #548: THE HEADING SAID "THE PLACEMENT DISCRIMINATOR" AND X IS NOT ONE -- MEASURED.
  A bound moved into DEVICE_TICK reads X=1 and PASSES: the FREEZE's LOAD write sets the
  bound to 1 before the drain counts, so any clamp outside the callback collapses the
  backlog first.  gate 2 (diff_fbpending_bound.c R1/R2/R5/R6) catches that placement.
  What X DOES catch is a bound gated on a control bit its CTRL=0 write clears:
  TIMER_ENABLE, which gate 2 -- every arm of it enabled -- cannot see, and TIMER_FCLK_256,
  which gate 2's unprescaled cases catch too.  X is the only RUNG-3 witness of either. ***

ARMS
  D  enabled throughout.  Backlog after T seconds.
  X  disabled during the accrual.  Backlog after T seconds.  Q1's discriminator.
  T  negative control: MMU left on, so the footbridge is not mapped.  Must not return
     the device's answers.  (machine_cats.c's boot page tables map only the low 256 MB;
     the footbridge is at PA 0x42000000.)
  H1/H2 harness legs (#545) -- they REPLACED arm K, a read of IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR (+0x18c) that
     the device used to answer with fatal() then exit(1); #545 declines it.  H1: an unknown -E
     name -- no prompt anywhere in the buffer, the diagnostic naming it, exit 1.  H2: arm D's prologue verified (21 steps, r3/r5/r6/r7),
     a fresh reg, quit, then one more reg that must fail with `dead` set BEFORE close(), exit 0.
     Every surviving arm must reap exit 0.  NOT COVERED: moving s.close() ahead of that next
     send keeps H2 green (the send fails on the closed descriptor), and the legs cannot defend
     their own predicates -- harness edits, reviewed, not rowed (footbridge_sites_probe.py).

Every RAM output word is poisoned with 0xDEADBEEF first and every verdict row requires
the values it consults to be PRESENT.  Every STEPPED instruction is checked, as it
EXECUTES, against the emulator's own disassembly (the hand-assembled-encoding trap): the
21-word prologue, and arm D's drain body (0x70-0xe4) in arm E.  *** #548: THIS SAID "EVERY
PLANTED INSTRUCTION".  The accrual loop (0x54-0x6c) and arm X's disable and re-enable
words (0x54/0x58, 0x70/0x74) FREE-RUN unchecked, and on a correct build nothing proves X
was disabled at all -- it reads 3, like D.  Filed as `fbdrainxdisable`. ***

usage: fbpending_drain_probe.py <gxemul-binary> <four-byte-raw-stub>
         [--accrue S] [--expect-cap N] [--timer N] [--fast] [--only ARM] [--learn]
"""
import os, pty, re, select, signal, struct, sys, time

BIN = sys.argv[1]
STUB = sys.argv[2]
LEARN = "--learn" in sys.argv


def _opt(name, default, cast):
    if name in sys.argv:
        return cast(sys.argv[sys.argv.index(name) + 1])
    return default


ACCRUE_S = _opt("--accrue", 4.0, float)
EXPECT_CAP = _opt("--expect-cap", -1, int)   # -1 = report only
#  WHICH of the four timers.  dev_footbridge.c derives timer_nr as
#  (relative_addr >> 5) & 3, so timer N's registers sit at 0x300 + N*0x20 and
#  TIMER_1_* is INDEX 0.  A fix applied to only one of the four callbacks passes a
#  row that exercises only timer 1, which is why this is a parameter and not a
#  constant.
TIMER_NR = _opt("--timer", 0, int)
TBASE = 0x300 + 0x20 * TIMER_NR
T_LOAD, T_VALUE, T_CTRL, T_CLEAR = TBASE, TBASE + 4, TBASE + 8, TBASE + 12

CODE = 0x00008000
DATA = 0x00009000
FB = 0x42000000

EMU_HZ = 50000000.0
#  --fast drives the counter at the machine's FULL emulated_hz (load 1, no
#  prescaler = 50 MHz on cats), which is the only setting that reaches the 2^31
#  wrap inside a run: 2^31 / 50e6 = 42.95 s.  The default 762.94 Hz would need
#  32 days.  The freeze/drain machinery below is independent of the accrual rate.
FAST = "--fast" in sys.argv
LOAD_RUN = 0x001 if FAST else 0x100   # with FCLK_256 -> 50e6/65536 = 762.94 Hz
LOAD_FREEZE = 0x00FFFFFF              # with FCLK_256 -> 0.0116 Hz
CTRL_ON = 0x80 if FAST else 0x88      # TIMER_ENABLE [| TIMER_FCLK_256]
CTRL_OFF = 0x00
SENTINEL = 0x00FFFFFF                 # random() % 0xFFFFFF can never produce it
SPIN = 0x00010000                     # 65536 iterations ~ 20 device-tick periods
LIMIT = 200000
RATE = EMU_HZ / (LOAD_RUN * (1.0 if FAST else 256.0))
ONLY = _opt("--only", "", str)        # run a single arm, e.g. --only X

VENDOR = 0x1011
LOADBACK = 0x00345678
POISON = 0xDEADBEEF

# ---- RAM map (offsets from DATA) -----------------------------------------
K_LIVE, K_SRC, K_L, K_FRZ, K_CON, K_SEN, K_SPIN, K_COFF = (
    0x00, 0x04, 0x08, 0x0c, 0x10, 0x14, 0x18, 0x1c)
O_DRAIN, O_ITER, O_LASTV, O_VEND, O_PHASE, K_LIM = (
    0x20, 0x24, 0x28, 0x2c, 0x30, 0x34)

CONSTS = {K_LIVE: 0x11223344, K_SRC: 0x12345678, K_L: LOAD_RUN,
          K_FRZ: LOAD_FREEZE, K_CON: CTRL_ON, K_SEN: SENTINEL,
          K_SPIN: SPIN, K_COFF: CTRL_OFF, K_LIM: LIMIT}
OUTS = (O_DRAIN, O_ITER, O_LASTV, O_VEND, O_PHASE)

R = dict(r0=0, r1=1, r2=2, r3=3, r4=4, r5=5, r6=6, r7=7, r8=8, r9=9,
         sl=10, fp=11, ip=12, sp=13, lr=14, pc=15)

282:    emit(0xe0, st("r1", "r4", O_PHASE), "PHASE = 4 -- drain limit hit")
283:    p.append((0xe4, br(0xE, CODE + 0xd8, CODE + 0xe4)[0], None, "b HALT"))
284:    return p
285:
286:
287:DRAIN_ENTRY = CODE + 0x70
288:PROLOGUE_N = 21          # 0x00 .. 0x50 inclusive
289:
290:
291:# ------------------------------------------------------------------ session
470:def plant(s, prog):
471:    for off, v in CONSTS.items():
472:        s.send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + off, v))
473:    for off in OUTS:
474:        s.send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + off, POISON))
475:    for off, word, _t, _c in prog:
476:        s.send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + off, word))
477:    s.send("pc=0x%08x" % CODE)
478:
479:
480:def spell_ok(expected, got):
481:    """The emulator's own spelling differs cosmetically from the generator's:
482:    it prints `ldr r5,[r4]` for a zero offset and `#0` rather than `#0x0`.  Those
483:    are FORMAT differences, not encoding differences -- the WORD is compared
484:    separately and exactly.  Normalise both sides so the check keeps its teeth
485:    (a wrong register or a wrong offset still fails) without red rows for
486:    punctuation."""
487:    def norm(t):
488:        t = re.sub(r"\s+", "", t.lower())
489:        t = re.sub(r",#0\]", "]", t)                      # [r4,#0]  -> [r4]
490:        t = re.sub(r"#0x([0-9a-f]+)",
491:                   lambda m: "#" + str(int(m.group(1), 16)), t)   # #0x100 -> #256
492:        return t
493:    return norm(expected) == norm(got)
494:
495:
496:def step_and_check(s, prog, n):
497:    planted = {CODE + off: (w, t) for off, w, t, _ in prog}
498:    bad, covered, ex = [], set(), []
499:    for _ in range(n):
500:        mark = len(s.buf)
501:        if not s.send("step 1", timeout=30) or s.dead:
502:            bad.append("host died / no prompt at step %d" % (len(ex) + 1))
503:            break
504:        m = re.search(r"([0-9a-f]{8}):\s+([0-9a-f]{8})\s+([^\r\n<]+)", s.buf[mark:])
505:        if not m:
506:            bad.append("no step line at step %d" % (len(ex) + 1))
507:            break
508:        addr, word = int(m.group(1), 16), int(m.group(2), 16)
509:        got = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", m.group(3)).strip()
510:        ex.append((addr, word, got))
511:        if addr not in planted:
512:            bad.append("stepped 0x%08x, never planted" % addr)
513:            continue
514:        pw, pt = planted[addr]
515:        covered.add(addr)
516:        if word != pw:
517:            bad.append("0x%08x read back 0x%08x, planted 0x%08x" % (addr, word, pw))
518:        if pt is not None and not spell_ok(pt, got):
519:            bad.append("0x%08x %08x -> %r, expected %r" % (addr, word, got, pt))
520:    return bad, covered, ex
521:
522:
523:def run_arm(label, mmu_on=False, disable=False, accrue=None, max_drain_s=30.0):
524:    accrue = ACCRUE_S if accrue is None else accrue
525:    prog = build(mmu_on=mmu_on, disable_during_accrual=disable)
526:    s = Session()
527:    if not s.wait(timeout=120):
528:        return dict(label=label, status="NO-PROMPT", **s.close())
529:    plant(s, prog)
530:    bad, cov, ex = step_and_check(s, prog, PROLOGUE_N)
531:    #  ACCRUAL.  The SIGALRM timer is stopped at the debugger prompt
532:    #  (debugger.c:800) and restarted on `continue` (:840), so only free-run
533:    #  seconds accrue -- which is what makes rate*T predictable at all.
534:    ok, dt = s.free_run(accrue)
535:    accrued = dt
536:    mid = s.read_words(DATA + O_DRAIN, 5) if ok and not s.dead else None
537:    #  PHASE CHANGE: the harness, not a guest clock, decides when accrual ends.
538:    if ok and not s.dead:
539:        s.send("pc=0x%08x" % DRAIN_ENTRY)
540:    #  DRAIN, in slices, until PHASE says done.
541:    t0, phase, out = time.time(), None, None
542:    while ok and not s.dead and time.time() - t0 < max_drain_s:
543:        ok, _ = s.free_run(0.5)
544:        if not ok or s.dead:
545:            break
546:        out = s.read_words(DATA + O_DRAIN, 5)
547:        if out is None:
548:            break
549:        phase = out[4]
550:        if phase in (3, 4):
551:            break
552:    r = s.regs()
553:    status = "HOST-DIED" if s.dead else ("SURVIVED" if ok else "INTERRUPT-LOST")
554:    end = s.close()
555:    return dict(label=label, status=status, regs=r, badis=bad, executed=ex,
556:                covered=cov, want={CODE + o for o, *_ in prog},
557:                accrued=accrued, mid=mid, out=out, phase=phase,
558:                drain_s=time.time() - t0, **end)
559:
560:
561:#  Arm E walks the drain body in SEGMENTS, each entered by setting pc directly.
562:#
563:#  WHY SEGMENTS AND NOT ONE WALK, stated because the first draft did the obvious
564:#  thing and got 7 offsets of silent non-coverage: under `step 1` the emulated CPU
565:#  advances ONE cycle per command, and DEVICE_TICK is driven by the dyntrans cycle
566:#  counter at one call per 1 << DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_TICK_SHIFT == 16384 cycles.  So a
567:#  single-stepped SPIN loop can never see a delivered tick inside a few dozen steps
568:#  and the TICKSEEN arm is unreachable by stepping, however large the backlog is.
569:#
570:#  This check is about DECODE, not about control flow: it asks whether the emulator
571:#  reads back each planted word as the instruction the assembler above meant.  That
572:#  question is answered wherever the word executes, so entering a segment at its head
573:#  is legitimate here -- and it is NOT a substitute for the behavioural evidence,
574:#  which is separate: reaching phase 3 with a non-zero drained count is only possible
575:#  if 0xac..0xc4 and 0xc8..0xd4 executed for real, in order, during arms D and X.
576:E_SEGMENTS = ((0x70, 12),        # re-enable NOPs, freeze, PHASE=1, DLOOP, SPIN head
577:              (0xa0, 2),         # subs fp / bne SPIN
578:              (0xa8, 1),         # b DONE -- the budget-expiry arm
579:              (0xac, 7),         # TICKSEEN .. b DLOOP
580:              (0xc8, 5),         # DONE .. HALT
581:              (0xdc, 2),         # OVER
582:              (0xe4, 1))         # b HALT
583:
584:
585:def run_drainenc():
586:    """Arm E: every word of the drain body stepped as it EXECUTES."""
587:    prog = build()
588:    s = Session()
589:    if not s.wait(timeout=120):
590:        return dict(status="NO-PROMPT", badis=[], covered=set(), nsteps=0,
591:                    executed=[], **s.close())
592:    plant(s, prog)
593:    bad, cov, ex = step_and_check(s, prog, PROLOGUE_N)
594:    for head, n in E_SEGMENTS:
595:        if s.dead:
596:            break
597:        s.send("pc=0x%08x" % (CODE + head))
598:        b2, c2, e2 = step_and_check(s, prog, n)
599:        bad += b2
600:        cov |= c2
601:        ex += e2
602:    status = "HOST-DIED" if s.dead else "SURVIVED"
603:    end = s.close()
604:    return dict(status=status, badis=bad, covered=cov, nsteps=len(ex), executed=ex, **end)
605:
606:
607:
608:
609:#  #545: THE HARNESS LEGS, replacing arm K.  Arm K read +0x18c, which the device used to answer with
610:#  fatal()+exit(1); #545 declines that read, so the control that "this launcher notices a dead child"
611:#  now comes from the harness itself:
612:#    H1  an unknown -E name: machine.c prints `Sorry, emulation "<name>" is unknown.` and main.c
613:#        exits 1 BEFORE any prompt.  Rowed on NO prompt AND the diagnostic naming THIS input AND
614:#        WIFEXITED && == 1 -- five different startup refusals all exit 1 with no prompt (measured,
615:#        pass 1), so only the text clause discriminates.
616:#    H2  the prologue stepped to its end, a FRESH `reg` with r3 bit 0 clear, r5 = 0x11223344,
617:#        r6 = VENDOR and r7 = LOADBACK (it really ran), `quit`, then ONE MORE ordinary `reg`: it
618:#        must fail with `dead` set BEFORE close(), and close() must reap exit 0.  A child dead
619:#        BEFORE the prologue finished shows `next send fails` + `dead` IDENTICALLY (measured), so
620:#        the fresh-reg values and exit == 0 are both load-bearing.
621:H1_NAME = "no-such-machine-fbd5"
622:
623:
624:def run_legs():
625:    #  H1
626:    s = Session(emul=H1_NAME)
627:    prompt = s.wait(timeout=120)
628:    end = s.close()
629:    h1 = dict(prompt=prompt, noprompt=("GXemul>" not in s.buf),
630:              named=('Sorry, emulation "%s" is unknown' % H1_NAME) in s.buf, **end)
631:    #  H2
632:    prog = build()
633:    s = Session()
634:    if not s.wait(timeout=120):
635:        return dict(h1=h1, h2=dict(status="NO-PROMPT", **s.close()))
636:    plant(s, prog)
637:    bad, cov, ex = step_and_check(s, prog, PROLOGUE_N)
638:    r = s.regs((3, 5, 6, 7))
639:    prologue_ok = (not bad and len(ex) == PROLOGUE_N and 3 in r and (r[3] & 1) == 0
640:                   and r.get(5) == 0x11223344 and r.get(6) == VENDOR and r.get(7) == LOADBACK)
641:    quit_rc = s.send("quit", timeout=10)
642:    next_rc = s.send("reg", timeout=10)          # observed BEFORE close(); never short-circuited
643:    dead_before_close = s.dead
644:    end = s.close()
645:    h2 = dict(status="SURVIVED" if prologue_ok else "PROLOGUE-FAILED", prologue_ok=prologue_ok,
646:              quit_rc=quit_rc, next_rc=next_rc, dead_before_close=dead_before_close, **end)
647:    return dict(h1=h1, h2=h2)

786:    #  the first draft of this file reported it as 3,735,928,559 pending interrupts.
787:    #  0xDEADBEEF in that word means the guest never executed the publish store,
788:    #  i.e. the very first poll found the sentinel intact: DEVICE_TICK delivered
789:    #  NOTHING.  That is the OPPOSITE failure from an unbounded backlog and it is the
790:    #  one this whole round is about, so conflating them would have shipped a wrong
791:    #  record about the exact symptom under test.  Both still FAIL; they just have to
792:    #  fail by their own names.  A live fix can never land here: the reset leaves the
793:    #  counter at 1, so at least one tick is always owed.  ***
794:    def backlog(A, ran):
795:        if not ran:
