98:/*
99: *  #442: the counter above was incremented without a bound, so a guest that armed
100: *  a timer and never acked overflowed it -- and on overflow the int goes negative,
101: *  DEVICE_TICK's `> 0` is false, and THE EMULATED CLOCK DELIVERS NOTHING FOR 2^31
102: *  FURTHER TICKS.  Reproduced at rung 3 on an unmodified -E cats: two MMIO writes,
103: *  then 42.95 s to the wrap and a 40 s dead window, measured three times.
104: *
105: *  THE COMPARISON PRECEDES THE INCREMENT, and that is not a style choice.  An
106: *  increment-then-check form -- which is what the one in-tree precedent for this
107: *  (dev_luna88k.c:252) actually does -- reintroduces the UB it is meant to remove
108: *  if the counter ever reaches INT_MAX, because the ++ has already executed by the
109: *  time the check runs.  luna88k is safe only because its own reset keeps the
110: *  counter far below INT_MAX; the FORM is still the wrong one to copy.
111: *
112: *  Reset to 1 rather than 0, so one interrupt is still owed -- coalescing the debt
113: *  rather than discarding it.  MEASURED to matter: a reset-to-0 mutant survives the
114: *  rung-3 drain probe and two of the three offline rows; only the trough row
115: *  separates them (0 against 1).
116: *
117: *  NO MESSAGE FROM HERE.  These run in the SIGALRM handler and debugmsg() is
118: *  printf underneath, so copying luna88k's diagnostic would put four printf paths
119: *  inside a signal handler -- and timer.c:274-279 states that nothing in that file
120: *  prints from the handler.  (luna88k's own callback does, which is a defect there;
121: *  filed, not fixed here.)  If a diagnostic is ever wanted, latch a flag here and
122: *  report from DEVICE_TICK, which runs in the main loop.
123: */
124:static void footbridge_pending(struct footbridge_data *d, int nr)
125:{
126:	if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] >= d->pending_bound[nr])
127:		d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] = 1;
128:	else
129:		d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] ++;
130:}
131:
132:static void timer_tick0(struct timer *t, void *extra)
133:{ footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 0); }
134:static void timer_tick1(struct timer *t, void *extra)
135:{ footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 1); }
136:static void timer_tick2(struct timer *t, void *extra)
137:{ footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 2); }
138:static void timer_tick3(struct timer *t, void *extra)
139:{ footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 3); }
242:	if (!with_prescaler)
243:		return cycles;
244:
245:	/*  The prescaler scales the PERIOD.  It must not be a signed shift, and the order
246:	    matters: TIMER_EXTERNAL is FCLK_16|FCLK_256, so test the pair first or 0x0C reads
247:	    as a bare FCLK_16 by luck of the `else if`.  */
248:	if ((control & (TIMER_FCLK_16 | TIMER_FCLK_256))
249:	    == (TIMER_FCLK_16 | TIMER_FCLK_256)) {
250:		/*  TIMER_EXTERNAL (0x0C): a LEGAL encoding, and this is now DATASHEET-BACKED
251:		    rather than inferred from the header.  The 21285 datasheet obtained for this
252:		    round gives TimerNControl bits 3:2 as 00=fclk_in, 01=fclk_in/16,
253:		    10=fclk_in/256, 11=External event (SS 7.3.41, p. 7-51; SS 6.4, p. 6-11 lists
254:		    the same four).  It used to reach fatal()+exit(1) below, so a CONFORMING guest
255:		    selecting the external clock source killed the host -- the same defect class as
256:		    the divide and arguably likelier, being a documented mode rather than a
257:		    malformed value.  The RATE an external source would give depends on a pin this
258:		    model has no signal for (irq_in_l), so it is genuinely not derivable here and
259:		    is NOT invented: the unprescaled period keeps the timer sane and the emulator
260:		    alive.  Modelling the external event properly is filed.  */
261:		;
262:	} else if (control & TIMER_FCLK_16)
263:		cycles *= 16;
264:	else if (control & TIMER_FCLK_256)
265:		cycles *= 256;
266:
267:	return cycles;
268:}
269:
270:
271:static void reload_timer_value(struct cpu *cpu, struct footbridge_data *d,
272:	int timer_nr)
273:{
274:	double freq = (double)cpu->machine->emulated_hz;
275:
276:	freq /= (double) footbridge_effective_cycles(d->timer_load[timer_nr],
277:	    d->timer_control[timer_nr], 1);
278:
279:	d->timer_value[timer_nr] = d->timer_load[timer_nr];
280:
281:	/*
282:	 *  #442: one emulated second of THIS timer's delivered rate, clamped.
283:	 *
284:	 *  The ceiling is DERIVED, not chosen: TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP ticks per timer
285:	 *  per signal at TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY signals per second is the most the
286:	 *  timer core can ever deliver to one timer in one wall second, so a cap
287:	 *  at that value can never throttle a legitimately fast timer.
288:	 *
289:	 *  IT IS ALSO NOT OPTIONAL, which a purely rate-relative bound hides.
290:	 *  With `-I 2147483647` and load 1 the requested rate is 2147483647.0,
291:	 *  so (int)freq is INT_MAX: a debt of 2^31-1 ticks, 31.5 s at the core's
292:	 *  full delivery rate -- NOT an overflow, as this said until #548: the
293:	 *  compare precedes the ++ (R2 measured green at peak INT_MAX).  Deleting
294:	 *  these two lines is the smallest edit still passing the rung-3 probe.
295:	 *
296:	 *  Floor 1.  *** ITS ORIGINAL JUSTIFICATION WAS MEASURED FALSE and is
297:	 *  corrected here rather than quietly dropped: *** the comment claimed a
298:	 *  0.0116 Hz timer "would otherwise bound at 0 and the counter would be
299:	 *  pinned there".  With the floor PRESENT that case already reads peak 1,
300:	 *  and with the floor DELETED the harness output is BYTE-IDENTICAL -- a
301:	 *  bound of 0 and a bound of 1 both pin at 1.  The floor prevents nothing
302:	 *  today; it is kept as a guard against a future bound expression that
303:	 *  could go negative, and that is the honest reason.
304:	 *
305:	 *  The comparisons are negated, but NOT for the reason first written.
306:	 *  timer_clamp_freq() puts its FLOOR first deliberately (timer.c:80-82:
307:	 *  "the FLOOR is tested FIRST so a NaN lands SLOW rather than fast"), and
308:	 *  this tests the CEILING first -- the inverse.  Harmless, because NaN is
309:	 *  UNREACHABLE here: emulated_hz is an int (machine.h:191) and
310:	 *  footbridge_effective_cycles() is >= 1 by construction, so this is
311:	 *  finite / finite-nonzero.  The claim to "match timer_clamp_freq's idiom"
312:	 *  was false in the one respect that idiom is about.
313:	 *
314:	 *  Set before timer_add()/timer_update_frequency() below.  *** THE REASON
315:	 *  FIRST GIVEN HERE -- that a SIGALRM landing between them would find a
316:	 *  zero bound -- IS NOT LOAD-BEARING, measured. ***  On the FIRST arm the
317:	 *  timer is not in first_timer until timer_add() links it, and timer.c
318:	 *  publishes fully-initialised (next stored before first_timer); on later
319:	 *  arms the OLD bound is still a valid bound.  A mutant moving this store
320:	 *  below the switch is a ZERO-BYTE object delta and passes every row.
321:	 *
322:	 *  Kept in this order because it reads correctly and costs nothing, not
323:	 *  because it closes a window.  Note also that this and the DEVINIT seed
324:	 *  are REDUNDANT WITH EACH OTHER -- either alone removes the zero-bound
325:	 *  state -- and neither comment used to mention the other doing the work.
326:	 */
327:	{
328:		double bound = freq;
329:		if (!(bound <= (double)TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY))
330:			bound = (double)TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY;
331:		if (!(bound >= 1.0))
332:			bound = 1.0;
333:		d->pending_bound[timer_nr] = (int) bound;
334:	}
335:
336:	/*  printf("%i: %i -> %f Hz\n", timer_nr,
337:	    d->timer_load[timer_nr], freq);  */
338:
339:	if (d->timer[timer_nr] == NULL) {
340:		switch (timer_nr) {
341:		case 0:	d->timer[0] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick0, d); break;
342:		case 1:	d->timer[1] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick1, d); break;
343:		case 2:	d->timer[2] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick2, d); break;
344:		case 3:	d->timer[3] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick3, d); break;
345:		}
346:	} else {
347:		timer_update_frequency(d->timer[timer_nr], freq);
348:	}
349:}
350:
351:
352:/*
353: *  The 4 footbridge timers should decrease and cause interrupts. Periodic
354: *  interrupts restart as soon as they are acknowledged, non-periodic
355: *  interrupts need to be "reloaded" to restart.
356: *
357: *  TODO: Hm. I thought I had solved this, but it didn't quite work.
358: *        This needs to be re-checked against documentation, sometime.
359: */
360:DEVICE_TICK(footbridge)
361:{
362:	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
363:	int i;
364:
365:	for (i=0; i<N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++) {
366:		if (d->timer_control[i] & TIMER_ENABLE) {
367:			if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[i] > 0) {
368:				/*  #432: the same never-zero span, WITHOUT the
369:				    prescaler -- this is a 24-bit counter, and the
370:				    prescaler slows the rate rather than widening
371:				    it.  */
372:				d->timer_value[i] = random() %
373:				    footbridge_effective_cycles(d->timer_load[i],
374:				    d->timer_control[i], 0);
375:				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->timer_irq[i]);
376:			}
377:		}
378:	}
379:}
380:
381:
382:/*
383: *  footbridge_interrupt_assert():
384: */
385:void footbridge_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
655:		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
656:			d->fiq_enable &= ~idata;
657:		break;
658:
659:	case TIMER_1_LOAD:
660:		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
661:			odata = d->timer_load[timer_nr];
662:		else {
663:			d->timer_load[timer_nr] = idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL;
664:			reload_timer_value(cpu, d, timer_nr);
665:			/*  debug("[ footbridge: timer %i (1-based), "
666:			    "value %i ]\n", timer_nr + 1,
667:			    (int)d->timer_value[timer_nr]);  */
668:			INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
669:		}
670:		break;
671:
672:	case TIMER_1_VALUE:
673:		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
674:			odata = d->timer_value[timer_nr];
675:		else
676:			d->timer_value[timer_nr] = idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL;
677:		break;
678:
679:	case TIMER_1_CONTROL:
680:		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
681:			odata = d->timer_control[timer_nr];
682:		else {
683:			d->timer_control[timer_nr] = idata;
684:			/*  #432: both prescaler bits set is TIMER_EXTERNAL
685:			    (0x0C, dc21285reg.h:364) -- a LEGAL named encoding,
686:			    and it used to reach fatal()+exit(1) right here, so a
687:			    guest selecting the external clock source KILLED THE
688:			    HOST.  Arguably likelier than the zero load, since it
689:			    is a documented mode rather than a malformed value.
690:			    The rate an external clock would actually give is
691:			    UNKNOWN and is NOT invented: footbridge_effective_cycles()
692:			    now gives the encoding a defined, survivable meaning
693:			    (the unprescaled period), and the real semantics are
694:			    filed for a round that has a datasheet.  */
695:			if (idata & TIMER_ENABLE) {
696:				reload_timer_value(cpu, d, timer_nr);
697:			} else {
698:				d->pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] = 0;
699:			}
700:			INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
701:		}
702:		break;
703:
704:	case TIMER_1_CLEAR:
705:		/*  #474: a READ must not perform the clear.  This case carried no
706:		    writeflag test at all, while TIMER_1_CONTROL immediately above it
707:		    does -- so a guest READ reloaded the counter, decremented the
708:		    pending count and DEASSERTED the interrupt.  A guest that merely
709:		    read the register silently acknowledged a pending timer interrupt;
710:		    the guest-visible consequence is a LOST interrupt, not a bad value.
711:
712:		    FOUR addresses, not one: the fold above normalises 0x30C/0x32C/
713:		    0x34C/0x36C onto this single case.
714:
715:		    GUARD THE BODY, KEEP THE break.  An early return here would skip
716:		    the function's common tail, which writes odata back on a read, and
717:		    would hand a guest an untouched buffer -- a defect this code does
718:		    not currently have.  */
719:		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
720:			if (d->timer_control[timer_nr] & TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC) {
721:				reload_timer_value(cpu, d, timer_nr);
722:			}
723:
724:			if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] > 0) {
725:				d->pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] --;
726:			}
727:
728:			INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
729:		}
730:		break;
#!/bin/bash
# GATE 14 -- ARM subtract-family carry flags (#311) and undefined routing (#312).
#
# What this protects
# -----------------
# #311: the subtract family computed its carry-out as `a >= b`. That is
# NOT-BORROW for SUB/CMP/RSB, which have no borrow-in, and WRONG for SBC/RSC,
# whose result is `a - b - NOT(C)`. The two disagree exactly when `a == b` with
# carry-in clear: `a - a - 1` borrows, so carry must be CLEAR, and `a >= b`
# reported it SET. The fix takes the flag from the full 64-bit result the file
# already computes (`c32 == c64`), which is the same answer as `a >= b` for the
# three instructions that were already right.
#
# #312: ARM's permanently-undefined encodings reached `goto bad`, which sets
# cpu->running = 0 (cpu_dyntrans.c) -- a legal-to-attempt instruction stopping
# the whole emulator, the same halt class as #264/#309/#310. They now raise the
# Undefined Instruction exception the silicon raises.
#
# This gate is known to be able to FAIL
# ------------------------------------
# It was run against the committed pre-fix build and scored 39 of the 56 rows it
# had at that moment, failing in exactly the 17 places the three fixes were
# predicted to flip and nowhere else. The two `subs rn=pc` pins were added after
# that sweep and pass either way, so the same build on today's table would read
# 41/58 -- stated exactly rather than restated as 39/58, because the count is
# evidence and evidence does not get rounded to the number that sounds better.
# That measurement is what shows these rows discriminate; gate 3's mutant
# machinery does not reach here, because it operates on float_emul.c and this
# gate measures the real emulator instead of a pure function.
#
# Swept again for #346 against a binary built from the parent commit: 162 of
# 165, failing the three cache-clean rows the fix flips and nowhere else. The
# two cache-clean CONTROL rows pass on both builds, which is what they are for.
#
# And again for #347 against a binary built from ITS parent (be6ea08): 168 of
# 173, failing exactly the five `A cclean2 ... ` discriminators and nowhere
# else. All three of that round's control/anti-clobber pins pass on both builds.
#
# And for round 99 (#348/#349/#350) against a binary built from the pre-fix
# HEAD (dea2fef): 181 of 190, failing exactly the NINE new DISC rows -- the
# two flags rows, the tail/zero/tail-V flag rows, the two fires rows,
# `A cacheclean nonmult` reading "fold", and `A cacheclean zero n` reading
# "none" -- and nowhere else. On the fixed build: 190/190. The retired
# `A cclean2 still fold` control asserted the MISSING flags and was replaced
# in the same change by the #349 marker rows, exactly as its own comment
# required. The nonmult witness's first draft is itself a recorded lesson:
# it expected a data abort at the top of RAM (measured false -- non-existent
# reads log and continue) and compared a dump string unswapped, so it read
# its own sentinel's parity; see run_cc_nonmult's docstring. Its "live"
# verdict was then tightened in review to also demand load progress (r0
# moved off 0x9120), closing a false pass against a guard that returns
# without calling the load.
#
# Why most of this gate is PINs
# ----------------------------
# #311 rewrites ONE line that five instructions share, and two of them were
# already correct. The pins are the point: they assert that SUB, RSB and CMP --
# and the ADD/ADC arm of the same #if, which keeps its own `c32 != c64` test --
# were not disturbed. A gate for this fix that checked only SBC and RSC would
# not detect the regression that matters most.
#
# The decode-time row
# ------------------
# `udf cond-failed nop` is the strongest reachability statement here: a
# conditional UDF whose condition is FALSE also stopped the committed
# emulator, because `goto bad` fires during DECODE, before any condition is
# evaluated. The guest never had to execute the instruction at all. That row
# asserts execution continued AND no exception was raised as a SINGLE value,
# because checking "no exception" alone would score a point on a build that
# halted before reaching anything.
set -u
cd "$(dirname "$0")"
. ./lib.sh

gate_begin "gate 14: ARM/Thumb flags (#311/#312/#319-#322/#328/#329)"

PMAX=${PMAX:-$ROOT/build/gxemul}
LOG=$LOGDIR/gate_arm.log
STUB=$LOGDIR/arm_stub.bin

need_exec "$PMAX"
command -v python3 >/dev/null || gate_skip "python3 not available"

# `testarm` prints usage and exits without a file argument, so a four-byte raw
# stub is loaded purely to make the machine construct. Every row overwrites it
# before anything executes; it is scaffolding, not code under test.
python3 -c "
import struct, sys
open(sys.argv[1], 'wb').write(struct.pack('<I', 0xe1a00000))
" "$STUB" || gate_skip "could not write the load stub"

note "binary : $PMAX"
note "machine: testarm/SA1110, cold debugger, RAM at 0"
note "little-endian; every word read is byte-swapped back by the probe"
note "flags read by the guest's own mrs r3,cpsr -- not a debugger register print"

python3 arm_flags_probe.py "$PMAX" "$STUB" > "$LOG" 2>&1 || true

if ! grep -q "ARM_FLAGS_RESULT=" "$LOG"; then
    note "probe produced no result line; last lines follow"
    tail -5 "$LOG" | sed 's/^/       /'
    gate_skip "probe did not complete"
fi

grep -E " ok$| FAIL$" "$LOG" | sed 's/^/       /'

# A probe whose control row failed has measured NOTHING and no other row may be
# believed. This harness has been bitten by a probe that reported a defect off
# sentinel reads while its guest code had never run.
ctrl=$(grep -o "ARM_FLAGS_CONTROL=[A-Z]*" "$LOG" | tail -1 | cut -d= -f2)
check "control row proves the probe measures" "${ctrl:-missing}" "OK"

res=$(grep -o "ARM_FLAGS_RESULT=[0-9]*/[0-9]*" "$LOG" | tail -1 | cut -d= -f2)
got=${res%/*}; want=${res#*/}
check "rows run"     "$want" 190
check "rows correct" "$got"  "$want"

# Both classes must stay populated: a gate that is all pins cannot detect a
# reverted fix, and one with no pins cannot detect collateral damage. The
# floors track the deltas each round adds; a review seat noted they are far
# below the actuals (112/78 after round 99) and so guard only against mass
# reclassification, with `rows run` guarding deletion.
disc=$(grep -c " DISC " "$LOG")
pins=$(grep -c " PIN " "$LOG")
check_min "discriminating rows present" "$disc" 58
check_min "pinned rows present"         "$pins" 66

# Named rows, one contract each, so a single site reverting cannot hide behind
# a total. Two spaces after the name: the probe pads names to a fixed column,
# and a name that is a PREFIX of another would otherwise match both rows (the
else
    grep -E "^  (ok|FAIL) " "$PITLOG" | sed 's/^/       /'

    #  THE THREE CONTROLS PLUS THE MMU-ON NEGATIVE CONTROL, each pinned by name.  A count of
    #  green rows would let a control go red while the total stayed acceptable.
    for c in "C1 liveness" "C2 MMU state" "A1 device signature" "T  MMU-ON control"; do
        check "pit8253 control: $c" \
              "$(grep -c "^  ok   $c" "$PITLOG")" "1"
    done

    #  Per-row presence.  A row that silently stops being tested is the padded-column class,
    #  so each is checked individually rather than by a total.  Eighteen rows: 4 controls +
    #  E/A2 apparatus + R0..R11.
    for v in "E  encodings" "A2 recompute stream" \
             "R0 the run produced data" "R1 latch preserves programming" \
             "R2 latch is not an error" "R3 latched read is not the reload" \
             "R4 16-bit readback is the reload" "R5 one recompute per write pair" \
             "R6 no transient count of zero" \
             "R7 read and write flip-flops are separate" \
             "R8 the latch is released by reading it" \
             "R9 a mid-pair latch completes the pair and lets go" \
             "R10 a Control Word rewinds the write flip-flop" \
             "R11 ...and it does so PER COUNTER, not just counter 0"; do
        check "pit8253 row green: $v" \
              "$(grep -c "^  ok   $v" "$PITLOG")" "1"
    done

    #  The denominator against a LITERAL: a probe that loses rows cannot pass by shrinking.
    check "pit8253 rows run" "$(grep -cE '^  (ok|FAIL) ' "$PITLOG")" "18"
fi

#  ---- xscaleinten: arm_coproc_i80321_6's read of the modelled inten register (CP6 crm 0 / crn 0)
#  ended the host with `fatal(); exit(1)' after computing the right value; it is now an ACCEPT.  The
#  promoted detector is 30 rows: the agy witness's 26, codex's two (D_OP1, INTEN_WRITE_PRESERVES_ISTEER),
#  D_CRM1_TMR0 (the crm-isolation row), and RD0_ALLONES, added on pass 2 after an exit keyed on
#  `rd == 0' measured 29/29 against the 29-row instrument.  Kill table 3
#  (artifacts/xscaleinten/killtable3_20260912/): twelve legs, all on prediction, BASE 30/30, HEAD 12/30;
#  each pass-2 row the SOLE killer of its own mutant (RD0_ALLONES kills M_RD0 and M_MAGICBIT).  Unreached rows report
#  BLOCKED and are not passes, so both FAIL and BLOCKED must be zero.  Section shape from the codex
#  promotion design (codex_promote_20260912b.txt), which said 28; pinned by hand at 30.
XSCALELOG=$LOGDIR/gate_xscale_inten.log
xrc=0
python3 -u xscale_inten_probe.py "$PMAX" gate "$STUB" > "$XSCALELOG" 2>&1 || xrc=$?

#  PARSE BEFORE SCORE: exactly one valid record of each kind.
if [ "$(grep -c '^XSCALE_RESULT=' "$XSCALELOG")" != "1" ] ||
   ! grep -Eq '^XSCALE_RESULT=(0|[1-9][0-9]*)/(0|[1-9][0-9]*)$' "$XSCALELOG" ||
   [ "$(grep -c '^XSCALE_CONTROL=' "$XSCALELOG")" != "1" ] ||
   ! grep -Eq '^XSCALE_CONTROL=(OK|FAIL)$' "$XSCALELOG" ||
   [ "$(grep -cx 'XSCALE_PROBE_DONE' "$XSCALELOG")" != "1" ]; then
    note "xscale probe produced no unique, valid completion record; last lines follow"
    tail -5 "$XSCALELOG" | sed 's/^/       /'
    check "xscale probe output parses" "no" "yes"
else
    check "xscale probe output parses" "yes" "yes"
    grep -E ' (ok|FAIL|BLOCKED)$' "$XSCALELOG" | sed 's/^/       /'
    check "xscale controls" "$(sed -n 's/^XSCALE_CONTROL=//p' "$XSCALELOG")" "OK"
    check "xscale probe exit status" "$xrc" "0"
    xres=$(sed -n 's/^XSCALE_RESULT=//p' "$XSCALELOG")
    #  Pinned by hand to a LITERAL: a pin that follows the probe cannot tell gained rows from lost ones.
    #  UNQUOTED on purpose: check_score_claims.py's PIN regex cannot see a quoted count, so `#526'
    #  shipped this as "30" and the score-claims check guarded nothing here (fixed in `#527').
    check "xscale rows run"     "${xres#*/}" 30
    check "xscale rows correct" "${xres%/*}" "30"
    check "xscale rows emitted" "$(grep -cE ' (ok|FAIL|BLOCKED)$' "$XSCALELOG")" "30"
    check "xscale rows failed"  "$(grep -c ' FAIL$' "$XSCALELOG")" "0"
    check "xscale rows blocked" "$(grep -c ' BLOCKED$' "$XSCALELOG")" "0"
fi

gate_end

 succeeded in 320ms:
#  IT LIVES HERE, in the ARM gate, for the same reason m8820x_sites_probe.py lives in
#  gate_m88k_rounding.sh: `cats` is an ARM machine and this gate already writes the four-byte
#  raw stub that makes such a machine construct.  A new gate script would move GATE_MANIFEST,
#  which is a bigger claim than one probe is worth.
#
#  THE TRAP IT GUARDS, and it nearly produced a confident wrong answer.  machine_cats.c:131
#  maps only the low 256 MB and the footbridge is at PA 0x42000000, so under the boot-time MMU
#  every device row reads 0x0 WITH A RAM LIVENESS ROW STILL GREEN -- which is exactly what an
#  absent device looks like.  The probe's arm T runs the same program with the MMU-disable
#  instructions replaced by nops and requires that it CANNOT produce the device's answer; arm L
#  reads CP15 c1 back and requires bit 0 clear.  Negative-controlled in both directions.
FBLOG=$LOGDIR/footbridge_sites.log
python3 footbridge_sites_probe.py "$PMAX" "$STUB" > "$FBLOG" 2>&1 || true
grep -E "^(  arm |C[0-9]|A[0-9]|I1|H[12]|D1|E(C1|A1|[0-6])|S(C1|A1|[1-5]|F[12])|N[0-5O]) " "$FBLOG" | sed 's/^/       /'

if ! grep -q "FOOTBRIDGE_SITES_" "$FBLOG"; then
    note "footbridge probe produced no verdict; last lines follow"
    tail -5 "$FBLOG" | sed 's/^/       /'
    check "footbridge probe completed" "no" "yes"
else
    #  THE CONTROL FIRST, and separately from the verdict.  A probe whose controls failed has
    #  measured NOTHING, and this one has a control that a liveness row cannot substitute for.
    check "footbridge: controls prove the probe measures" \
          "$(grep -c 'FOOTBRIDGE_SITES_CONTROL=OK' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    check "footbridge: every row green" \
          "$(grep -c 'FOOTBRIDGE_SITES_PASS' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    #  VALUES, NOT SURVIVAL.  0x1011 is DC21285_VENDOR_ID (dev_footbridge.c `case VENDOR_ID`)
    #  and is a MODEL value: real hardware packs DeviceID:VendorID into a word read at +0x0,
    #  so the round that fixes packed config reads changes this pin in the same commit.  It can only
    #  have come back through this device; an unmapped read survives too, it just returns 0.
    check "footbridge: VENDOR_ID 0x1011 through a real guest ldr" \
          "$(grep -cE '^A1 .*got=0x00001011 .*ok$' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    #  The vanishing top byte IS the handler's signature: `case TIMER_1_LOAD`'s write branch stores
    #  idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL.
    #  RAM, or any decode that merely absorbed the store, returns 0x12345678 unchanged.
    check "footbridge: TIMER_1_LOAD masked to 24 bits by the handler" \
          "$(grep -cE '^A2 .*got=0x00345678 .*ok$' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    #  THE PROBE'S OWN FAILABILITY CONTROLS -- #545 replaced the kill bridge (arm K, a +0x18c read
    #  the device used to answer with exit(1); #545 declines it) with two HARNESS legs, pinned by
    #  their DISCRIMINATING tokens, not by the rows existing:
    #    H1 an unknown -E name: NO prompt, the diagnostic NAMING the input, WIFEXITED && == 1.
    #       Five different startup refusals all exit 1 with no prompt, so the text is the pin.
    #    H2 quit after a VERIFIED prologue (fresh-reg values), the next send dead BEFORE
    #       teardown, reaped exit 0 -- a child dead before the prologue shows `dead` identically,
    #       so the prologue clause and exit 0 are both required, and both are in the row.
    check "footbridge: H1 startup leg green (unknown -E named, exit 1)" \
          "$(grep -cE '^H1 .*named=True .*ok$' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    check "footbridge: H1's diagnostic names the rejected input" \
          "$(grep -c 'Sorry, emulation "no-such-machine-fbd5" is unknown' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    check "footbridge: H2 quit leg green (prologue verified, dead, exit 0)" \
          "$(grep -cE '^H2 .*prologue=True next=False dead=True exit=0 .*ok$' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    #  #545: the five declined sites, ONE process (arm S).  N0 counts five DISTINCT once-announces
    #  (a shared or aggregate latch reads 4 or 1); N1-N5 count each site's text over the whole arm and NO
    #  requires the announces in site order (ORDER, not emitter); S1-S5 and SF1/SF2 pin every
    #  interrupt register unchanged through the declines, with the FINAL raw reads after the last write
    #  of every pair.  Every family is pinned by its DISTINCT green ids (a duplicated green line cannot
    #  stand in for a missing id -- pass 2), and the probe's own total by ROWS=.
    check "footbridge: the sites probe ran all 37 rows green (ROWS=37 of 37)" \
          "$(grep -cE '^ROWS=37 of 37$' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    check "footbridge: arm S controls SC1, SA1 -- two DISTINCT green ids" \
          "$(grep -E '^S(C1|A1) .*ok$' "$FBLOG" | cut -d' ' -f1 | sort -u | wc -l | tr -d ' ')" "2"
    check "footbridge: N0 five sites, one process, five distinct once" \
          "$(grep -cE '^N0 .*distinct-once=5 fatals=5 exit=0 .*ok$' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    check "footbridge: NO the five announces arrive in access order" \
          "$(grep -cE '^NO .*order=N1,N2,N3,N4,N5 .*ok$' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    check "footbridge: N1-N5 each announced exactly once (count=1)" \
          "$(grep -cE '^N[1-5] .*count=1 .*ok$' "$FBLOG")" "5"
    check "footbridge: N1-N5 are five DISTINCT green row ids" \
          "$(grep -E '^N[1-5] .*count=1 .*ok$' "$FBLOG" | cut -d' ' -f1 | sort -u | wc -l | tr -d ' ')" "5"
    check "footbridge: S1-S5 the legal reads between the declines -- five DISTINCT green ids" \
          "$(grep -E '^S[1-5] .*ok$' "$FBLOG" | cut -d' ' -f1 | sort -u | wc -l | tr -d ' ')" "5"
    check "footbridge: S3 IRQEnable intact at 0x30 through the declines" \
          "$(grep -cE '^S3 .*got=0x00000030 .*ok$' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    check "footbridge: SF1/SF2 final raw status reads -- two DISTINCT green ids, both 0" \
          "$(grep -E '^SF[12] .*got=0x00000000 .*ok$' "$FBLOG" | cut -d' ' -f1 | sort -u | wc -l | tr -d ' ')" "2"
    #  #545: the corrected controls -- C4 now requires the key present; C5 requires arm T's exit 0.
    check "footbridge: C4 the MMU-on arm cannot see the device (key present)" \
          "$(grep -cE '^C4 .*ok$' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    check "footbridge: C5 the MMU-on control arm survives, exit 0" \
          "$(grep -cE '^C5 .*ok$' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    #  #539: IRQEnable (+0x188) is readable -- reset 0, Set ORs, Clear clears, byte and
    #  halfword reads return the low lanes -- and arm E raises no footbridge diagnostic.
    #  Seven value/text rows, pinned by count AND by the two that carry the datasheet claims.
    check "footbridge: IRQEnable ACCEPT rows E0-E6 all green" \
          "$(grep -cE '^E[0-6] .*ok$' "$FBLOG")" "7"
    #  Seven green lines are not seven rows: a duplicated id could stand in for a missing one, so
    #  the DISTINCT ids are counted OVER THE GREEN LINES -- seven distinct green ids proves
    #  "each of E0-E6 is green" by itself (fable5, #539 F2: counting ids over all lines would
    #  pass a duplicated green E2 beside a red E3).
    check "footbridge: E0-E6 are seven DISTINCT green row ids" \
          "$(grep -E '^E[0-6] .*ok$' "$FBLOG" | cut -d' ' -f1 | sort -u | wc -l | tr -d ' ')" "7"
    check "footbridge: IRQEnable reads 0 at reset (E0)" \
          "$(grep -cE '^E0 .*got=0x00000000 .*ok$' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    check "footbridge: Set ORs (E2 = P|Q; Clear is pinned by E5)" \
          "$(grep -cE '^E2 .*got=0x60002002 .*ok$' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    #  The defence against the hand-assembled-encoding trap: every planted word is compared
    #  against the disassembly the emulator printed AS IT STEPPED IT.
    check "footbridge: no planted word failed its disassembly check" \
          "$(grep -c 'DISASSEMBLY MISMATCH' "$FBLOG")" "0"
fi

#  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
#  #439/#440 PIT 8253 latch + LSB/MSB selector -- pitlatch / pitclobber.
#
#  WIRED HERE, ALONGSIDE footbridge_sites_probe.py, BECAUSE gate_hygiene.sh SAID SO.  Its
#  own 17 -> 18 bump note reads: "Wiring it into gate_arm.sh alongside
#  footbridge_sites_probe.py is the follow-up, and it wants an operator who can run that
#  gate."  The probe execs `-E cats` and hand-assembles ARM, so this is the only gate whose
#  machine it shares.
#
# everything.  Measured 2026-08-19: the true pre-#429 revert now fails 4 rows and the
# flipped comparison fails 5, both on VALUES rather than by crashing.
# ------------------------------------------------------------------ #442 fbpending
#  THE FOOTBRIDGE PENDING-INTERRUPT COUNTER, bounded.  Unbounded, it overflowed INT_MAX and
#  the emulated clock delivered NOTHING for 2^31 further ticks -- reproduced at rung 3 on an
#  unmodified -E cats, three runs, wrap at ~43 s and a 40 s dead window.
#
#  THIS ROW IS A DETECTOR, NOT THE REPRODUCTION, and the distinction is load-bearing here.
#  It #includes the shipped dev_footbridge.c and calls timer_tickN() directly, which the
#  witness ladder's mechanical discriminator classes as never-a-reproduction.  The
#  reproduction is regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py (rung 3) and it is NOT run here.
#
#  BOTH SHIP BECAUSE NEITHER ALONE IS ENOUGH -- measured, not assumed:
#    * the rung-3 probe cannot reach the boundary.  The core delivers at most
#      TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY = 68,157,440 increments per wall second, so
#      2^31 of them take 31.5 s AT BEST and a gate row cannot spend that.  This harness does
#      2^31 direct increments in ~4 s.
#    * a mutant deleting the absolute ceiling (leaving the bound at the timer's own rate)
#      PASSES the rung-3 probe on every timer and is caught only here, at emulated_hz above
#      68 MHz -- reachable through the documented -I flag.
#
#  It links every object EXCEPT main.o and dev_footbridge.o, so it runs the REAL timer.c and
#  the REAL footbridge rather than re-implementations.  Measured kills: pre-fix fails R1 x7
#  and R2 with trough -2147483648 (the overflow itself); reset-to-0 fails R3 x7; a fix applied
#  to one callback only fails R1 x7; no-ceiling fails R1 on the two high-hz cases and R2 with
#  peak 2147483647.
FBBIN=$LOGDIR/diff_fbpending_bound
FBLOG=$LOGDIR/diff_fbpending_bound.log
FBOBJS=$(find "$SEC/../build/src" -name '*.o' ! -name 'main.o' ! -name 'dev_footbridge.o' 2>/dev/null | sort)
if [ -z "$FBOBJS" ]; then
    note "fbpending bound: no build/ objects -- SKIPPED, not passed"
else
    if ! $CC -O2 -std=gnu99 -I"$SEC/src/include" -I"$SEC/src/include/thirdparty"             -I"$SEC/src/devices" -o "$FBBIN" "$HERE/diff_fbpending_bound.c"             $FBOBJS -lm -L/usr/X11R6/lib -lX11 > "$FBLOG" 2>&1; then
        note "fbpending bound compile failed:"; sed 's/^/       /' "$FBLOG" | head -12
        check "fbpending bound: links against the real timer.c" "no" "yes"
    else
        check "fbpending bound: links against the real timer.c" "yes" "yes"
        #  The depth argument is what turns R2 on.  Without it R2 SKIPS -- and a skipped row
        #  that reads as a pass is this project's fifth vacuity class, so the count below
        #  pins that R2 actually ran.
        "$FBBIN" 2147487744 > "$FBLOG" 2>&1
        sed 's/^/       /' "$FBLOG" | tail -20
        check "fbpending bound: offline verdict" "$(grep -c '^FBBOUND_PASS$' "$FBLOG")" "1"
        check "fbpending bound: R2 actually ran (not SKIPPED)"               "$(grep -c 'R2 no signed overflow.*direct ticks' "$FBLOG")" "1"
        check_min "fbpending bound: rows actually run"               "$(grep -cE '^  (ok|FAIL) ' "$FBLOG")" 15
    fi
fi

RTCBIN=$LOGDIR/diff_rtc_range
RTCLOG=$LOGDIR/diff_rtc_range.log
if ! $CC -O2 -std=gnu99 -I"$SEC/src/include" -I"$SEC/src/include/thirdparty" \
        -ffunction-sections -fdata-sections -Wl,--gc-sections \
        -o "$RTCBIN" "$HERE/diff_rtc_range.c" > "$RTCLOG" 2>&1; then
    note "RTC range differential compile failed:"; sed 's/^/       /' "$RTCLOG" | head -12
    check "RTC range: compiles against the real dev_rtc.c" "no" "yes"
else
    check "RTC range: compiles against the real dev_rtc.c" "yes" "yes"
    "$RTCBIN" > "$RTCLOG" 2>&1
    sed 's/^/       /' "$RTCLOG"
    check     "RTC range: row failures" \
              "$(grep -oE '[0-9]+ failures' "$RTCLOG" | grep -oE '^[0-9]+')" "0"
    #  Floor 9 -> 12 -> 17, once per closed escape, and the value is named here on purpose
    #  (this file records the sh4_tmu comment that said "16 -> 17" while the code read 18):
    #    9 -> 12  the clamp TARGET was asserted nowhere, so `d->hz = 1;` passed all nine.
    #    12 -> 17 the threshold was pinned only from ABOVE, and the timer core's own input
    #             was never read.  Both MEASURED at 12 of 12 green.
    check_min "RTC range: rows actually run" \
              "$(grep -oE '^[0-9]+ rows' "$RTCLOG" | grep -oE '^[0-9]+')" 18
    check     "RTC range: offline verdict" "$(grep -c 'RTC_RANGE_PASS' "$RTCLOG")" "1"
    #  NAMED ROWS, because each is the SOLE detector of one arm and the row-count floor
    #  cannot see a deletion that also drops the floor.  -F throughout: the row names carry
    #  `^`, which is literal here but is a metacharacter one careless quote away.
    #
    #  2^32 and 2^63 are the two values MEASURED to turn an ADD into a REMOVE before #429.
    #  bit31 is the third arm and the quietest: it survives as a NEGATIVE int, which the
    #  timer core's floor turns into roughly one tick per three years -- the guest asked
    #  for the fastest rate available and got the slowest.  The zero rows are the CONTROL
    #  in the other direction: zero is the documented way to stop the timer, so a fix that
    #  clamped it into something else would be a regression this table must refuse.
    check     "RTC range: the 2^32 truncation row is present" \
              "$(grep -c -F '2^32: timer NOT removed' "$RTCLOG")" "1"
    check     "RTC range: the 2^63 truncation row is present" \
              "$(grep -c -F '2^63: timer NOT removed' "$RTCLOG")" "1"
    check     "RTC range: the bit-31 sign row is present" \
              "$(grep -c -F 'bit31: hz is positive' "$RTCLOG")" "1"
    check     "RTC range: the zero-keeps-its-meaning rows are present" \
              "$(grep -c -F 'zero: ' "$RTCLOG")" "2"
    #  *** THE SOLE DETECTOR OF THE CLAMP TARGET, and the first nine rows did not have it.
    #  ***  Two panel seats said independently that no row asserts WHAT the clamp produces --
    #  only that it is nonzero, positive, and did not remove the timer.  MEASURED rather than
    #  predicted: `d->hz = (int) TIMER_MAX_FREQUENCY;` changed to `d->hz = 1;` passed 9 of 9.
    #  Deleting these three rows restores that hole, so they are named here.
    check     "RTC range: the clamp-exactness rows are present" \
              "$(grep -c -F 'clamped to exactly TIMER_MAX_FREQUENCY' "$RTCLOG")" "3"
    #  *** THE THRESHOLD WAS PINNED ONLY FROM ABOVE, AND THAT WAS A MEASURED FALSE PASS.
    #  ***  Until this row the table sampled ONE in-range value (1000), so the bound could
    #  be moved anywhere in [1000, INT_MAX] undetected.  The measure seat found the cheapest
    #  form: TIMER_MAX_FREQUENCY -> TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP, ONE IDENTIFIER, and the two macros sit
    #  two lines apart in timer.h -- 12 of 12 green while every guest rate above 2^20 was
    #  boosted to INT_MAX, which timer.h:72-77 itself calls unserviceable.
    check     "RTC range: the threshold is pinned from BELOW" \
              "$(grep -c -F 'above CATCHUP: passed through, NOT clamped' "$RTCLOG")" "1"
    check     "RTC range: the boundary value itself is tested" \
              "$(grep -c -F 'INT_MAX exactly: passed through, not clamped' "$RTCLOG")" "1"
    #  What the TIMER CORE is told, as opposed to what the device stored.  Deleting
    #  timer_update_frequency() passed 12 of 12: the detector captured last_timer_hz from the
    #  start and no row ever read it.
    check     "RTC range: the timer core's own input is asserted" \
              "$(grep -c -F 'timer core got' "$RTCLOG")" "2"
    #  THE CONSTBLIND GUARD.  The three clamp rows READ TIMER_MAX_FREQUENCY, so they are
    #  green for any value of it; this row asserts the ceiling IS INT_MAX, derived from
    #  outside timer.h.  Named because deleting it silently restores the blindness -- and
    #  because five seats voted to DROP the constblind row as "already fixed" while THIS
    #  file, added after that audit, was a live instance of it.
    check     "RTC range: the constant-blindness guard is present" \
              "$(grep -c -F 'ceiling IS INT_MAX' "$RTCLOG")" "1"
fi

# ---- #405: the ATA IDENTIFY capacity bytes --------------------------------------
# Third instance of the same construction (float_emul, dev_sh4, now dev_wdc): the driver
# stubs the diskimage_* externals and #includes dev_wdc.c, so the function that runs is
# the one that ships. wdc_initialize_identify_struct() is static, so there is no way to
# link it without including the file.
#
# *** WHY THE NAMED ROWS BELOW ARE THE WHOLE POINT. `% 255` and `& 255` return the SAME
# byte for every operand below 255, so a table of plausible-looking disk sizes passes on
# the UNFIXED code -- every disk under 33,423,360 bytes agrees. Each named row puts a
# specific byte at or past the divergence point. Measured against the shipped defect:
# the full revert fails 7 rows, and a PARTIAL fix that corrects only the two `>> 8`
# lines still fails 4 -- which is the case a less careful table would have let through.
# ***
WDCBIN=$LOGDIR/diff_wdc_identify
WDCLOG=$LOGDIR/diff_wdc_identify.log

        -ffunction-sections -fdata-sections -Wl,--gc-sections \

245:	/*  The prescaler scales the PERIOD.  It must not be a signed shift, and the order
246:	    matters: TIMER_EXTERNAL is FCLK_16|FCLK_256, so test the pair first or 0x0C reads
247:	    as a bare FCLK_16 by luck of the `else if`.  */

250:		/*  TIMER_EXTERNAL (0x0C): a LEGAL encoding, and this is now DATASHEET-BACKED
251:		    rather than inferred from the header.  The 21285 datasheet obtained for this
252:		    round gives TimerNControl bits 3:2 as 00=fclk_in, 01=fclk_in/16,
253:		    10=fclk_in/256, 11=External event (SS 7.3.41, p. 7-51; SS 6.4, p. 6-11 lists

255:		    selecting the external clock source killed the host -- the same defect class as
256:		    the divide and arguably likelier, being a documented mode rather than a
257:		    malformed value.  The RATE an external source would give depends on a pin this
258:		    model has no signal for (irq_in_l), so it is genuinely not derivable here and
259:		    is NOT invented: the unprescaled period keeps the timer sane and the emulator
260:		    alive.  Modelling the external event properly is filed.  */
261:		;
262:	} else if (control & TIMER_FCLK_16)
263:		cycles *= 16;
264:	else if (control & TIMER_FCLK_256)
265:		cycles *= 256;
266:
267:	return cycles;

271:static void reload_timer_value(struct cpu *cpu, struct footbridge_data *d,
272:	int timer_nr)
273:{
274:	double freq = (double)cpu->machine->emulated_hz;
281:	/*
282:	 *  #442: one emulated second of THIS timer's delivered rate, clamped.
283:	 *
284:	 *  The ceiling is DERIVED, not chosen: TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP ticks per timer
285:	 *  per signal at TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY signals per second is the most the
286:	 *  timer core can ever deliver to one timer in one wall second, so a cap
287:	 *  at that value can never throttle a legitimately fast timer.
288:	 *
289:	 *  IT IS ALSO NOT OPTIONAL, which a purely rate-relative bound hides.
290:	 *  With `-I 2147483647` and load 1 the requested rate is 2147483647.0,
291:	 *  so (int)freq is INT_MAX: a debt of 2^31-1 ticks, 31.5 s at the core's
292:	 *  full delivery rate -- NOT an overflow, as this said until #548: the
293:	 *  compare precedes the ++ (R2 measured green at peak INT_MAX).  Deleting
294:	 *  these two lines is the smallest edit still passing the rung-3 probe.
295:	 *
296:	 *  Floor 1.  *** ITS ORIGINAL JUSTIFICATION WAS MEASURED FALSE and is
297:	 *  corrected here rather than quietly dropped: *** the comment claimed a
298:	 *  0.0116 Hz timer "would otherwise bound at 0 and the counter would be
299:	 *  pinned there".  With the floor PRESENT that case already reads peak 1,
300:	 *  and with the floor DELETED the harness output is BYTE-IDENTICAL -- a
301:	 *  bound of 0 and a bound of 1 both pin at 1.  The floor prevents nothing
302:	 *  today; it is kept as a guard against a future bound expression that
303:	 *  could go negative, and that is the honest reason.
304:	 *
305:	 *  The comparisons are negated, but NOT for the reason first written.
306:	 *  timer_clamp_freq() puts its FLOOR first deliberately (timer.c:80-82:
307:	 *  "the FLOOR is tested FIRST so a NaN lands SLOW rather than fast"), and
308:	 *  this tests the CEILING first -- the inverse.  Harmless, because NaN is
309:	 *  UNREACHABLE here: emulated_hz is an int (machine.h:191) and
310:	 *  footbridge_effective_cycles() is >= 1 by construction, so this is
311:	 *  finite / finite-nonzero.  The claim to "match timer_clamp_freq's idiom"
312:	 *  was false in the one respect that idiom is about.
313:	 *
314:	 *  Set before timer_add()/timer_update_frequency() below.  *** THE REASON
315:	 *  FIRST GIVEN HERE -- that a SIGALRM landing between them would find a
316:	 *  zero bound -- IS NOT LOAD-BEARING, measured. ***  On the FIRST arm the
317:	 *  timer is not in first_timer until timer_add() links it, and timer.c
318:	 *  publishes fully-initialised (next stored before first_timer); on later
319:	 *  arms the OLD bound is still a valid bound.  A mutant moving this store
320:	 *  below the switch is a ZERO-BYTE object delta and passes every row.
321:	 *
322:	 *  Kept in this order because it reads correctly and costs nothing, not
323:	 *  because it closes a window.  Note also that this and the DEVINIT seed
324:	 *  are REDUNDANT WITH EACH OTHER -- either alone removes the zero-bound
325:	 *  state -- and neither comment used to mention the other doing the work.
326:	 */
327:	{
328:		double bound = freq;
329:		if (!(bound <= (double)TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY))
330:			bound = (double)TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY;
331:		if (!(bound >= 1.0))
332:			bound = 1.0;
333:		d->pending_bound[timer_nr] = (int) bound;
334:	}
335:
336:	/*  printf("%i: %i -> %f Hz\n", timer_nr,
337:	    d->timer_load[timer_nr], freq);  */
338:
339:	if (d->timer[timer_nr] == NULL) {
340:		switch (timer_nr) {
341:		case 0:	d->timer[0] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick0, d); break;
342:		case 1:	d->timer[1] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick1, d); break;
343:		case 2:	d->timer[2] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick2, d); break;
344:		case 3:	d->timer[3] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick3, d); break;
345:		}
346:	} else {
347:		timer_update_frequency(d->timer[timer_nr], freq);
348:	}
349:}

#!/bin/bash
# GATE 14 -- ARM subtract-family carry flags (#311) and undefined routing (#312).
#
# What this protects
# -----------------
# #311: the subtract family computed its carry-out as `a >= b`. That is
# NOT-BORROW for SUB/CMP/RSB, which have no borrow-in, and WRONG for SBC/RSC,
# whose result is `a - b - NOT(C)`. The two disagree exactly when `a == b` with
# carry-in clear: `a - a - 1` borrows, so carry must be CLEAR, and `a >= b`
# reported it SET. The fix takes the flag from the full 64-bit result the file
# already computes (`c32 == c64`), which is the same answer as `a >= b` for the
# three instructions that were already right.
#
# #312: ARM's permanently-undefined encodings reached `goto bad`, which sets
# cpu->running = 0 (cpu_dyntrans.c) -- a legal-to-attempt instruction stopping
# the whole emulator, the same halt class as #264/#309/#310. They now raise the
# Undefined Instruction exception the silicon raises.
#
# This gate is known to be able to FAIL
# ------------------------------------
# It was run against the committed pre-fix build and scored 39 of the 56 rows it
# had at that moment, failing in exactly the 17 places the three fixes were
# predicted to flip and nowhere else. The two `subs rn=pc` pins were added after
# that sweep and pass either way, so the same build on today's table would read
# 41/58 -- stated exactly rather than restated as 39/58, because the count is
# evidence and evidence does not get rounded to the number that sounds better.
# That measurement is what shows these rows discriminate; gate 3's mutant
# machinery does not reach here, because it operates on float_emul.c and this
# gate measures the real emulator instead of a pure function.
#
# Swept again for #346 against a binary built from the parent commit: 162 of
# 165, failing the three cache-clean rows the fix flips and nowhere else. The
# two cache-clean CONTROL rows pass on both builds, which is what they are for.
#
# And again for #347 against a binary built from ITS parent (be6ea08): 168 of
# 173, failing exactly the five `A cclean2 ... ` discriminators and nowhere
# else. All three of that round's control/anti-clobber pins pass on both builds.
#
# And for round 99 (#348/#349/#350) against a binary built from the pre-fix
# HEAD (dea2fef): 181 of 190, failing exactly the NINE new DISC rows -- the
# two flags rows, the tail/zero/tail-V flag rows, the two fires rows,
# `A cacheclean nonmult` reading "fold", and `A cacheclean zero n` reading
# "none" -- and nowhere else. On the fixed build: 190/190. The retired
# `A cclean2 still fold` control asserted the MISSING flags and was replaced
# in the same change by the #349 marker rows, exactly as its own comment
# required. The nonmult witness's first draft is itself a recorded lesson:
# it expected a data abort at the top of RAM (measured false -- non-existent
# reads log and continue) and compared a dump string unswapped, so it read
# its own sentinel's parity; see run_cc_nonmult's docstring. Its "live"
# verdict was then tightened in review to also demand load progress (r0
# moved off 0x9120), closing a false pass against a guard that returns
# without calling the load.
#
# Why most of this gate is PINs
# ----------------------------
# #311 rewrites ONE line that five instructions share, and two of them were
# already correct. The pins are the point: they assert that SUB, RSB and CMP --
# and the ADD/ADC arm of the same #if, which keeps its own `c32 != c64` test --
# were not disturbed. A gate for this fix that checked only SBC and RSC would
# not detect the regression that matters most.
#
# The decode-time row
# ------------------
# `udf cond-failed nop` is the strongest reachability statement here: a
# conditional UDF whose condition is FALSE also stopped the committed
# emulator, because `goto bad` fires during DECODE, before any condition is
# evaluated. The guest never had to execute the instruction at all. That row
# asserts execution continued AND no exception was raised as a SINGLE value,
# because checking "no exception" alone would score a point on a build that
# halted before reaching anything.
set -u
cd "$(dirname "$0")"
. ./lib.sh

gate_begin "gate 14: ARM/Thumb flags (#311/#312/#319-#322/#328/#329)"

PMAX=${PMAX:-$ROOT/build/gxemul}
LOG=$LOGDIR/gate_arm.log
STUB=$LOGDIR/arm_stub.bin

need_exec "$PMAX"
command -v python3 >/dev/null || gate_skip "python3 not available"

# `testarm` prints usage and exits without a file argument, so a four-byte raw
# stub is loaded purely to make the machine construct. Every row overwrites it
# before anything executes; it is scaffolding, not code under test.
python3 -c "
import struct, sys
open(sys.argv[1], 'wb').write(struct.pack('<I', 0xe1a00000))
" "$STUB" || gate_skip "could not write the load stub"

note "binary : $PMAX"
note "machine: testarm/SA1110, cold debugger, RAM at 0"
note "little-endian; every word read is byte-swapped back by the probe"
note "flags read by the guest's own mrs r3,cpsr -- not a debugger register print"
    done

    #  The denominator against a LITERAL: a probe that loses rows cannot pass by shrinking.
    check "pit8253 rows run" "$(grep -cE '^  (ok|FAIL) ' "$PITLOG")" "18"
fi

#  ---- xscaleinten: arm_coproc_i80321_6's read of the modelled inten register (CP6 crm 0 / crn 0)
#  ended the host with `fatal(); exit(1)' after computing the right value; it is now an ACCEPT.  The
#  promoted detector is 30 rows: the agy witness's 26, codex's two (D_OP1, INTEN_WRITE_PRESERVES_ISTEER),
#  D_CRM1_TMR0 (the crm-isolation row), and RD0_ALLONES, added on pass 2 after an exit keyed on
#  `rd == 0' measured 29/29 against the 29-row instrument.  Kill table 3
#  (artifacts/xscaleinten/killtable3_20260912/): twelve legs, all on prediction, BASE 30/30, HEAD 12/30;
#  each pass-2 row the SOLE killer of its own mutant (RD0_ALLONES kills M_RD0 and M_MAGICBIT).  Unreached rows report
#  BLOCKED and are not passes, so both FAIL and BLOCKED must be zero.  Section shape from the codex
#  promotion design (codex_promote_20260912b.txt), which said 28; pinned by hand at 30.
XSCALELOG=$LOGDIR/gate_xscale_inten.log
xrc=0
python3 -u xscale_inten_probe.py "$PMAX" gate "$STUB" > "$XSCALELOG" 2>&1 || xrc=$?

#  PARSE BEFORE SCORE: exactly one valid record of each kind.
if [ "$(grep -c '^XSCALE_RESULT=' "$XSCALELOG")" != "1" ] ||
   ! grep -Eq '^XSCALE_RESULT=(0|[1-9][0-9]*)/(0|[1-9][0-9]*)$' "$XSCALELOG" ||
   [ "$(grep -c '^XSCALE_CONTROL=' "$XSCALELOG")" != "1" ] ||
   ! grep -Eq '^XSCALE_CONTROL=(OK|FAIL)$' "$XSCALELOG" ||
   [ "$(grep -cx 'XSCALE_PROBE_DONE' "$XSCALELOG")" != "1" ]; then
    note "xscale probe produced no unique, valid completion record; last lines follow"
    tail -5 "$XSCALELOG" | sed 's/^/       /'
    check "xscale probe output parses" "no" "yes"
else
    check "xscale probe output parses" "yes" "yes"
    grep -E ' (ok|FAIL|BLOCKED)$' "$XSCALELOG" | sed 's/^/       /'
    check "xscale controls" "$(sed -n 's/^XSCALE_CONTROL=//p' "$XSCALELOG")" "OK"
    check "xscale probe exit status" "$xrc" "0"
    xres=$(sed -n 's/^XSCALE_RESULT=//p' "$XSCALELOG")
    #  Pinned by hand to a LITERAL: a pin that follows the probe cannot tell gained rows from lost ones.
    #  UNQUOTED on purpose: check_score_claims.py's PIN regex cannot see a quoted count, so `#526'
    #  shipped this as "30" and the score-claims check guarded nothing here (fixed in `#527').
    check "xscale rows run"     "${xres#*/}" 30
    check "xscale rows correct" "${xres%/*}" "30"
    check "xscale rows emitted" "$(grep -cE ' (ok|FAIL|BLOCKED)$' "$XSCALELOG")" "30"
    check "xscale rows failed"  "$(grep -c ' FAIL$' "$XSCALELOG")" "0"
    check "xscale rows blocked" "$(grep -c ' BLOCKED$' "$XSCALELOG")" "0"
fi

gate_end

codex
The seven per-timer checks do not verify which timer ran: replacing `--timer "$t"` with `--timer 0` would still admit the same green logs for all four iterations. R6 also overwrites the precharge result without asserting it. Separately, PRETABLE’s control-failure channel is inaccurate: a failed control produces REPORT-ONLY, so the guard skips the later control check.

PRETABLE -- #548 fbdrainwire, written BEFORE the pass-2 kill table (its mtime is the order proof)
=========================================================================================

Scope: this round EXTENDS a kill-table-backed detector (gate 2's R6 in regress/diff_fbpending_bound.c) and SHIPS new
detector rows (gate 14's drain block in regress/gate_arm.sh).  Both are #442's detector material.  The pass-1 tables
(opus548_measured/) ran BEFORE the edit, against the drafted design; the pass-2 table runs against the final text.

1. SIBLING PARITY
   * drain block vs the footbridge_sites block beside it: controls first (CONTROL=OK) -- same; distinct GREEN ids
     (twelve control rows) -- same idiom as E0-E6 / N1-N5; a value pin (BOUNDED=YES (cap asserted: 762)) -- same role as
     the sites block's VENDOR_ID/24-bit-mask pins; a unique completion guard -- the sites block greps a verdict token,
     the drain block additionally requires PASS|FAIL (REPORT-ONLY is not completion) -- stricter, not asymmetric; exit
     status -- the sites block ignores it (`|| true`), the drain block pins it (xscale's idiom).  The sites block pins
     `DISASSEMBLY MISMATCH` = 0; the drain probe prints no such token (its mismatches print as
     "ENCODING / READBACK MISMATCH" and fail control row E), so the E id in the twelve carries that pin.  NO ROW OWED.
   * R6 vs R6b: R6b (timer 1 carries its own bound) is untouched; R6 gains timer 2's owed cell.  NO ROW OWED.

2. CLAIM -> ROW (every added sentence asserting an observable consequence)
   gate_arm.sh block
     "reads 3/3 on timer 0 and X=3051 on timer 1 ... passes BOTH gate-2 differentials"   M_en0: measured pass 1;
         rows: t1 verdict/BOUNDED/exit (the owed four-timer rows ARE the carrier).  RE-MEASURE on the final block.
     "CATCHES the bound REMOVED on any timer"             M_nobound: t0-t3 verdict/BOUNDED/exit rows.  RE-MEASURE.
     "a bound gated on ... TIMER_ENABLE, and TIMER_FCLK_256"  M_en, M_fclk: verdict rows via arm X.  RE-MEASURE.
     "any B >= N, which reads N"                          OWED A MEASUREMENT: no B>=N mutant was built in pass 1.
         Build M_x8 (`double bound = freq * 8.0;`, B = 6103 at 762.94 Hz) and show the verdict rows red.
     "NOT CLAIMED ..." / "cannot show at all" / "FILED as fbboundmodes"   disclosure, no row.
     "`==` ... until #548 gave R6 its second cell"        gate 2 R6: M_eq.  RE-MEASURE on the final file.
     "LOAD ... still read 1183"                           measured pass 1 (runs_p1/M_nobound_starve12_t0.out); no row.
   fbpending_drain_probe.py header
     "A bound moved into DEVICE_TICK reads X=1 and PASSES"   M_devtick, measured pass 1; a NOT-caught claim, no row.
     "gate 2 ... catches that placement" / "TIMER_FCLK_256, which gate 2's unprescaled cases catch too"
         gate 2 R1/R2/R5/R6 on M_devtick and M_fclk (runs_p1/diff_fbb_M_devtick.out, diff_fbb_M_fclk.out).
   diff_fbpending_bound.c R6 comment
     "one guest CONTROL write left it 4529 owed"          measured, runs_eq (the rung-3 re-arm driver).  No row.
     "Owing 4096 before timer 0's own re-arm would have TRADED the cells"   M_pendguard under R6new, measured.
   dev_footbridge.c / gate_offline.sh
     "R2 measured green at peak INT_MAX" / "R2 stays green at peak 2147483647, trough 1"
         runs_p1/diff_fbb_M_noceil.out.  No row (a correction of a false record).

3. GUARD FIELD vs BODY FIELD -- footbridge_pending()
   guard reads  pending_timer_interrupts[nr], pending_bound[nr]
   body writes  pending_timer_interrupts[nr]
   pending_timer_interrupts[] writers: the callback (:126-129), CONTROL without ENABLE (:698, = 0), TIMER_x_CLEAR
     (:724-725, -- when > 0), DEVINIT's memset.
   pending_bound[] writers: reload_timer_value (:333, on EVERY arm or re-arm, under no predicate), DEVINIT (:815, = 1).
   The two are written at DIFFERENT sites, so a state where they disagree is owed: count ABOVE the bound.  Reached by a
   re-arm that LOWERS the bound over an owed count (and by the drain probe's FREEZE, where no callback follows).
   OWED ROW: R6's second cell (timer 2 re-armed with 4096 owed) -- this round's R6both.  The first cell (count below
   the new bound) is the row R6 already had.

4. CHANNEL -- per row, the mutant it kills and the observable that differs under it
   gate 14, per timer t:
     "reached a verdict"        a probe that crashes, hangs to the harness budget, or prints REPORT-ONLY (a control
                                failed / no cap parsed) -> no unique PASS|FAIL line.
     "controls prove ..."       any control failing -> FBDRAIN_CONTROL=FAILED.
     "twelve DISTINCT green ids" a control row DELETED or renamed in the probe -> 11 ids (CONTROL=OK cannot see it).
     "bounded at the asserted cap 762"  M_nobound / M_en / M_fclk / M_x8 -> BOUNDED=NO; a probe edit that parses a
                                different cap -> "(cap asserted: N)" differs.
     "both clocks alive"        a zero-tick drain (poisoned count) -> "FBDRAIN_CLOCK_DEAD=D ..." (whole-line match).
     "verdict PASS"             every bound mutant above -> VERDICT=FAIL.
     "probe exit status"        VERDICT=FAIL or a failed control -> exit 1.
   Under M_nobound the verdict, BOUNDED and exit rows vary (3051 > 762).  No row's named channel is constant under
   its named mutant.
   gate 2 R6: M_eq -> timer 2 peak 8192; M_pendguard -> timer 0 peak 4096; M_frozen -> peak 4096/8192;
     M_idx0 -> R6 (peak 1) and R6b.

TABLE 2 (pass 2) OWES, on the FINAL text: R6both x the 15 pass-1 mutants (identity 28 rows); the gate-14 block,
extracted verbatim, x {base, M_nobound, M_en, M_en0, M_fclk, M_x8, M_eq, M_devtick, M_nper} on all four timers.

from pathlib import Path
import re, subprocess
root = Path.cwd()
art = root / 'regress/artifacts/fbdrainwire_20260924'
script = r'''
set -u
log="'$(cat -- "$1")
fdrc=0
n=0
fail=0
check() { n=$((n+1)); if [ "$1" != "$2" ]; then fail=$((fail+1)); fi; }
if [ "$(grep -c '"'"'^FBDRAIN_VERDICT='"' <<< \""'$log")" != "1" ] || ! grep -Eqx '"'FBDRAIN_VERDICT=(PASS|FAIL)' <<< \""'$log"; then
    printf '"'guard=reject\\n'
    exit 0
fi
check yes yes
check \""'$(grep -cx '"'FBDRAIN_CONTROL=OK' <<< \""'$log")" 1
check "$(grep -E '"'"'^  ok   (R0|C1|C2|A1|A2|E|H1|H2|T|L1|L2|P) '"' <<< \""'$log" | awk '"'{print "'$2}'"' | sort -u | wc -l | tr -d ' ')\" 12
check \""'$(grep -cx '"'FBDRAIN_BOUNDED=YES  (cap asserted: 762)' <<< \""'$log")" 1
check "$(grep -cx '"'FBDRAIN_CLOCK_DEAD=' <<< \""'$log")" 1
check "$(grep -cx '"'FBDRAIN_VERDICT=PASS' <<< \""'$log")" 1
check "$fdrc" 0
printf '"'checks=%s failures=%s\\n' \""'$n" "$fail"
'"'''
for name in ['fbpending_drain_t0.log', 'fbpending_drain_t1.log', 'fbpending_drain_t2.log', 'fbpending_drain_t3.log']:
    p = art / 'gate14_20260924' / name
    unix = '/c/' + p.as_posix()[3:]
    run = subprocess.run([r'C:\\Program Files\\Git\\bin\\bash.exe', '--noprofile', '--norc', '-c', script, 'review', unix], text=True, capture_output=True)
    print(name, run.stdout.strip(), 'shell_rc=', run.returncode, run.stderr.strip())
for n in [0, 762, 763, 3051, 3052]:
    b = 762
    normal = ((n-1) % b) + 1 if n else 0
    gt = ((n-1) % (b+1)) + 1 if n else 0
    reset0 = n % (b+1)
    print('recurrence only:', dict(N=n, base=normal, greater_than=gt, reset_to_0=reset0))
for name in ['diff_fbb_M_fclk.out', 'diff_fbb_M_noceil.out']:
    s = (art / 'opus548_measured/runs_p1' / name).read_text()
    failures = [line for line in s.splitlines() if re.match(r'"'^  FAIL '"', line) and '@@SELFCHECK@@' not in line]
    print(name, 'printed non-selfcheck failures=', len(failures), 'ids=', [line.split()[1] for line in failures])

"C:\\WINDOWS\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command "@'
from pathlib import Path
import re
root=Path('regress/artifacts/fbdrainwire_20260924')
def score(text):
    lines=text.splitlines()
    verdicts=[s for s in lines if s.startswith('FBDRAIN_VERDICT=')]
    if len(verdicts)"'!=1 or verdicts[0] not in ('"'FBDRAIN_VERDICT=PASS','FBDRAIN_VERDICT=FAIL'):
        return 'completion rejected; later checks skipped'
    ids={s.split()[1] for s in lines if re.match(r'"'^  ok   (R0|C1|C2|A1|A2|E|H1|H2|T|L1|L2|P) '"',s)}
    checks=[True, lines.count('FBDRAIN_CONTROL=OK')==1, len(ids)==12, lines.count('FBDRAIN_BOUNDED=YES  (cap asserted: 762)')==1, lines.count('FBDRAIN_CLOCK_DEAD=')==1, lines.count('FBDRAIN_VERDICT=PASS')==1, True]
    return f'{sum(checks)}/7 predicates (recorded exit 0)'
for t in range(4):
    s=(root/f'gate14_20260924/fbpending_drain_t{t}.log').read_text()
    print(f'archived timer {t}:',score(s))
s=(root/'gate14_20260924/fbpending_drain_t0.log').read_text()
print('timer-0 log under any iteration label:',score(s))
control_bad=s.replace('FBDRAIN_CONTROL=OK','FBDRAIN_CONTROL=FAILED').replace('FBDRAIN_VERDICT=PASS','FBDRAIN_VERDICT=REPORT-ONLY')
print('failed control:',score(control_bad))
s=(root/'opus548_measured/runs_p1/base_reportonly_t0.out').read_text()
print('archived REPORT-ONLY:',score(s))
print('timer-3 lowering: R5 max across [762,762,762,1] =',max([762,762,762,1]))
def drive(pending,bound,n,eq=False):
    samples=[]
    for _ in range(n):
        pending=1 if (pending==bound if eq else pending>=bound) else pending+1
        samples.append(pending)
    return pending,max(samples),min(samples)
for pre in [0,4096]:
    print('R6 recurrence only, pre=',pre,'HEAD(final,peak,trough)=',drive(pre,762,4096),'M_eq=',drive(pre,762,4096,True))
print('partial-positive reload guard: initial bound 50000000, pending 1, proposed bound 762; store?',not 1 or 1>=762)

codex
The no-ceiling correction is supported by the logs. Two broader claims need changes: reset-to-zero can be detected when the residue is zero, and R5’s maximum across four timers can hide a bound lowered on just one timer. The two-cell R6 change closes the measured `==` gap, but doesn’t establish complete re-arm coverage. Git Bash could not start in this sandbox; the remaining checks used source inspection and a Python replay of the log predicates.

import ast, subprocess
from pathlib import Path
s=subprocess.run(['git','show','HEAD:tools/pipeline/check_probe_wiring.py'],check=True,text=True,capture_output=True).stdout
node=next(n for n in ast.parse(s).body if isinstance(n,ast.Assign) and any(isinstance(t,ast.Name) and t.id=='EXEMPT' for t in n.targets))
e=ast.literal_eval(node.value)
print('HEAD exemptions:',len(e),'fbpending=',e['fbpending_drain_probe.py'])
del e['fbpending_drain_probe.py']
print('Proposed exemptions:',len(e),'dates=',sorted({v[0] for v in e.values()}))
p=Path('regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py')
t=ast.parse(p.read_text())
build=next(n for n in t.body if isinstance(n,ast.FunctionDef) and n.name=='build')
null_expected=[]
for n in ast.walk(build):
    if isinstance(n,ast.Tuple) and len(n.elts)==4 and isinstance(n.elts[0],ast.Constant) and isinstance(n.elts[2],ast.Constant) and n.elts[2].value is None:
        null_expected.append(hex(n.elts[0].value))
print('Instructions with no expected disassembly text:',', '.join(sorted(null_expected,key=lambda v:int(v,16))))
print('Stepped drain branches among them:',', '.join(v for v in sorted(null_expected,key=lambda v:int(v,16)) if int(v,16)>=0x70))

codex
VERDICT: SHIP WITH CHANGES

1. **The four-timer coverage is not pinned.** In the new [gate_arm.sh block](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_arm.sh), replacing `--timer "$t"` with `--timer 0` preserves all 28 checks while running timer 0 four times. None of the predicates reads timer identity. This restores the documented escape for M_en0. Deleting `3` from the loop also removes coverage without failing any remaining check.

   This is **outside NOT CLAIMED**. Pin the probe’s selected index and register base, and require four distinct timer identities across the section. The submitted invocation selects all four correctly; its checks do not defend that property.

2. **R6’s new precondition can disappear silently.** Change the first `drive(r6, 2, 4096, ...)` count to `0` in [diff_fbpending_bound.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/diff_fbpending_bound.c:299). The subsequent drive overwrites `peak2` and `trough2`, so baseline R6 and IDENTITY remain green, and M_eq again passes: both re-arms start at zero.

   This is another **undisclosed floor breach**. Preserve and assert the precharge result, particularly `pending_timer_interrupts[2] == 4096` immediately before re-arm. Also assert timer 0’s zero-count precondition.

3. **R6both closes the measured cases, not the whole re-arm state space.** A concrete additional mutation is to guard the bound assignment with:

   `pending == 0 || pending >= (int)bound`

   Here `pending` means this timer’s pending count and `bound` is the newly calculated bound. Both R6 cells still update correctly. A re-arm with `0 < pending < new_bound` incorrectly retains the old bound. The ordinary drain also misses this: X’s re-enable requests the existing bound, and FREEZE lowers it below the residue.

   This is a **derived escape requiring measurement**, outside the claimed R6 closure. Add a positive count below the new bound, or describe the coverage specifically as the measured zero-count and above-bound cases. “Below” versus “already past” also omits equality; the comparison partitions `< B` versus `>= B`. The documented M_eq/M_pendguard results themselves are supported.

4. **“All of those are gate 2’s” overstates protection against lowering.** R5 compares the **maximum** peak across four timers with the expected bound. One correctly bounded timer can therefore hide another timer’s lowered bound.

   A concrete candidate is changing the assignment index in [dev_footbridge.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_footbridge.c:333) from `[timer_nr]` to `[timer_nr % 3]`. Timer 3 retains its initial bound—zero in the calloc-based detector, one after device initialization—and consequently peaks at one. R5’s maximum remains correct; R6 never exercises timer 3; its drain remains positive and below 762.

   This escape is **derived, not a newly measured mutant result**. Lowering is disclosed for the drain, but the assertion that gate 2 covers it is too broad. Assert rate equality per timer, rather than only on their maximum.

5. **The new mathematical contract still contains false absolutes.**

   - The residue formula requires a zero starting count, positive fixed bound, `N >= 1`, no acknowledgments during accrual, and preservation through observation. At `N = 0`, the count is zero.
   - “Any `B >= N`” produces a cap failure only when **`N > 762`**. The load paragraph acknowledges this limitation, but the CATCHES sentence should state it.
   - **Reset-to-zero can show.** Its recurrence is `N mod (B + 1)`. For `B = 762`, `N = 3051` produces 762 and passes; `N = 3052` produces zero and fails the clock-alive check. “Cannot show at all” is false.
   - FREEZE explains the measured DEVICE_TICK mutant’s collapse. It does not explain every listed escape or establish a result for “any clamp outside the callback.”
   - The starvation measurements establish two passing baseline cases, not “never reds falsely.” Zero delivered ticks and finite harness deadlines remain failure paths.

   The stated condition for the `>` mutant is correct under the recurrence assumptions: it exceeds 762 exactly at positive multiples of 763.

6. **The corrected probe header still overclaims.** In [fbpending_drain_probe.py](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py:55), “A non-zero result proves … disabled” contradicts the newly disclosed absence of evidence that X was disabled. Changing X’s `disable=True` to `False` leaves a second enabled run and preserves the measured green result. This escape is **disclosed as `fbdrainxdisable`**, but the proof sentence must become conditional.

   “Every STEPPED instruction” also overstates semantic disassembly verification. Seven stepped drain branches have expected text `None`; `step_and_check()` verifies their encoded word but skips the disassembly-text comparison. State that distinction.

   Finally, restrict “only RUNG-3 witness” to the selected gate configuration. The probe also has a `--fast` configuration whose enabled arm clears FCLK_256; the packet does not establish global uniqueness across witness configurations.

7. **The option-set disclosure omits FCLK_16 and EXTERNAL.** Adding a condition that skips bounding whenever `TIMER_FCLK_16` is set would escape the shown callback-driving cases: their controls are `0x80`, `0x88`, and `0x00`. EXTERNAL includes that bit too.

   This is a **derived additional mode escape**. The general `fbboundmodes` filing may encompass it, but the committed NOT CLAIMED paragraph names only PERIODIC. Explicitly include these modes or add corresponding bound-detector cases.

8. **The shell is sound; PRETABLE’s channel attribution is not.** The quoted arguments and redirection are correct. `fdrc=0` with `|| fdrc=$?` preserves failures. The completion guard rejects missing, duplicate, malformed, and REPORT-ONLY verdicts. Basic `grep -cx` treats the cap’s parentheses literally; the empty CLOCK_DEAD whole-line match is correct; `$2` is the control ID, and `sort -u` prevents duplicates substituting for missing IDs.

   However, an ordinary failed control produces REPORT-ONLY. The guard records failure and executes `continue`, so **neither “controls prove …” nor the exit-status row executes**. Correct their named channels in PRETABLE. This does not create a false pass.

   The block also has no explicit timeout around Python. A permanently hung invocation cannot reach its completion check; the claimed “harness budget” behavior needs an identified external watchdog.

9. **The records are improved, but not entirely correct.** The device’s no-overflow correction and [gate_offline.sh correction](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_offline.sh:1323) agree with the raw no-ceiling log. `INT_MAX / 68,157,440 ≈ 31.5077` seconds is correct. However, deleting two ceiling lines is not “the smallest edit still passing the rung-3 probe”: the single-token `>=` → `==` mutation is already a measured counterexample. Say “a small edit.”

   The [checker paragraph](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/pipeline/check_probe_wiring.py) is accurate: HEAD has eleven exemptions; removing this entry leaves ten, all dated 2026-09-27.

   Two packet counts also need correction: [harness_matrix.txt](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbdrainwire_20260924/opus548_measured/harness_matrix.txt) contains **14 mutants plus baseline**, across four harness variants—60 runs, not fifteen mutants. M_fclk has **12 printed non-selfcheck FAIL rows**: eleven substantive failures plus aggregate R4.

10. **Verification and remaining measurements.** I checked scoped source, HEAD versions of the mixed working files, and the archived evidence. Python replay of the seven log predicates accepted each recorded baseline log and also accepted timer 0’s log regardless of iteration label. The counter examples above were checked algebraically. No gates, battery, or emulator mutants were run; Git Bash failed to initialize in the sandbox.

    The additional measurements worth requesting are the positive-below-bound re-arm guard, the timer-3 assignment-index mutation, and the FCLK_16/EXTERNAL condition. Compiled-object identity, the complete battery’s witness inventory, and the external timeout behavior remain reported premises rather than independently verified conclusions.

   The [checker paragraph](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/pipeline/check_probe_wiring.py) is accurate: HEAD has eleven exemptions; removing this entry leaves ten, all dated 2026-09-27.

   Two packet counts also need correction: [harness_matrix.txt](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbdrainwire_20260924/opus548_measured/harness_matrix.txt) contains **14 mutants plus baseline**, across four harness variants—60 runs, not fifteen mutants. M_fclk has **12 printed non-selfcheck FAIL rows**: eleven substantive failures plus aggregate R4.
